# TASKS.md — Kudos task board

Shared, lightweight task board for all AIs + the human. **Read [`AGENTS.md`](AGENTS.md) first.**
Update this file whenever you start, finish, or hand off work — it is the primary
handoff channel between sessions and between agents.

## How to use it (30 seconds)

- **Claim** a task → move it to **In Progress**; set `Owner`, `Branch`, status `🔄`.
- **Finish** → move it to **Completed** with the commit SHA(s) + date.
- **Hand off / block** → leave it in **In Progress** with `✋ HANDOFF` or `⛔ BLOCKED`
  and a clear **Next step** / **Blocker**.
- Keep entries short; link to `docs/…` or `READIUM_MIGRATION_NOTES.md` for detail.
- Task IDs are `T-NN` (just for cross-reference; pick the next free number).

**Status:** 🔄 in progress · ✋ handoff (ready for pickup) · ⛔ blocked · ✅ done · 🅿️ backlog

| T-187 | **iOS: shake-to-report requires direction reversals, not just a threshold.** `ShakeIntensityMonitor.shouldAcceptShake()` fired on peak acceleration alone; a single hard jolt (bump, pickup, set-down) could pass the same 1.65g threshold a deliberate shake does, with no backstop the way `UIWindow.motionShake`'s own OS heuristic partially provides on iOS. Added a pure `shakeSamplesReversed` (dot product of consecutive samples < 0, both above a noise floor) and a rolling reversal-timestamp window; acceptance now requires ≥2 reversals within 0.6s on top of the existing threshold. 3 new tests (`KudosTests/ShakeDetectorTests.swift`). **A2 (reader scrollbar):** grep found no suppression code, but **owner confirmed live on-device there is genuinely no system scrollbar** — the "trust the OS" assumption was wrong. No animation code is written: the system drives push, dismiss, and the interactive swipe-back. The app currently uses none of `matchedTransitionSource` / `navigationTransition` / `matchedGeometryEffect`. **Plan:** (1) a custom `EnvironmentKey` carrying a `Namespace.ID`, declared once per tab stack (`HomeView`, `LibraryView`, `SearchView`, `AccountView`) beside the existing `navigationDestination` registrations — the card and the destination are far apart in this codebase (cards in `WorkCoverCard` / `AO3WorkCoverCard` across many containers, destinations resolved centrally in `LocalWorkDestinationView`), and the environment is what gets one namespace to both ends; (2) attach the source to both cover cards; (3) attach the transition to the reader branch of `LocalWorkDestinationView`, and later to `.detail` for the ⓘ path if wanted. **Owner decision (explicit):** if the native transition replaces the hand-rolled drag-to-dismiss, that is accepted — `ReaderDismissDrag.swift` is 257 lines of UIKit peel-transform host whose interactive dismissal the zoom transition brings for free, so the adoption should *delete* code rather than layer on top of it. **Owner confirmed on device (2026-07-31): with the zoom in place the swipe-down still "feels hand-rolled" — the peel is still the thing driving dismissal, so it should come out.** Removing it is more than deleting a file: the drag is threaded `ReadiumReaderView` → `ReadiumNavigatorContainer` (`dismissSurface`, `onDismissInteractionActiveChange`, `onDismissDragEnded`) → `ReaderDismissPeelHost`, and `ReadiumBook` gates locator ingestion on it (`isDismissInteractionActive` / `isDismissExitLatched`, see `isLocatorIngestionBlocked`) so a drag cannot corrupt the flushed resume point. **The coupling that will bite:** the peel host sets `host.safeAreaRegions = []`, and both the page-box inset formula (T-185's bottom-margin fix) and `ReaderPageSkeleton`'s window-inset padding now depend on the reader being full-bleed. Whatever replaces the host has to keep zeroing those regions, or the reader regains the double-padding bug the surface's own comment documents. | T-175 | **On-device LLM for author's-note classification (owner's idea, parked 2026-07-31).** "Is this paragraph the author talking to the reader, or narration?" is semantic, and `AuthorNoteDetector`'s phrase table can only recognise what someone has already seen — every new PDF has taught it a few more phrases. Use **Apple's Foundation Models framework** (iOS 26, on-device, no bundled weights): bundling a quantized 0.5–3B GGUF is 300MB–2GB against the 6MB bar MuPDF was held to, so it is the only viable option. Owner's device is a 17 Pro, so Apple Intelligence is available and the feature is dogfoodable; macOS and ineligible devices keep the table as the floor. **Design constraints:** (1) it must be **additive only** — run on blocks the deterministic rules did *not* claim, so it can never demote prose the rules protected (the asymmetry the detector is built on); (2) verdicts should live **beside** the EPUB as annotations rather than baked into its markup, because an LLM breaks the reproducibility `converterVersion` assumes — same file, same version, different output, and Apple updates the model without asking — and annotations are correctable, which merges this with **T-158**'s user-editable notes; (3) the ~4k-token context rules out feeding a whole work, but notes live at chapter edges, so it is a handful of short blocks per chapter and can be deferred to the background. | unassigned | — | 🅿️ IDEA | **First step is a measurement, not an implementation:** point the on-device model at the blocks the table did *not* claim in "In the Service of the Queen" and compare. Real notes the rules missed → the feature justifies itself; broad agreement → the table is already at its ceiling for known sources and the value is only in novel ones. Throwaway harness, no app changes. A lower-cost alternative that keeps runtime deterministic: use a model as a **development** tool to propose candidate indicator phrases for a human to measure and add. |

Anything re-rendered outside one of those subtrees (what a dismissed presentation causes) falls back to the default. **Two layers:** the asset makes the compiled-in default AO3 red, covering every path including undiscovered ones; `ContentView.applyWindowTint` pushes the *current* `effectiveTint` to UIKit's window `tintColor`, since Sepia's warm brown and a user-chosen accent aren't knowable at build time and `tintColor` inherits down the UIKit hierarchy SwiftUI hosts render into. | Claude Opus 5 | `claude/non-epub-pdf-distribution-ab6eba` | ✅ DONE | Verified `AccentColor` compiles into `Assets.car` (`assetutil`); `Scripts/check-invariants.sh` now fails if either the asset or the build setting goes missing. | T-171 | **⚠️ Politeness debt: an automatic availability check needs a cheaper signal than one-request-per-work.** Owner's framing, and it is the right one: "100s of requests to AO3 for a large library just to see if works are still available is not very polite." T-170's sweep is therefore **manual only**. What would make it automatic: (1) a **cheap batch signal** — AO3 has no "what changed" feed, but a user's own Bookmarks/Marked-for-Later pages list many works per request, and a work missing from a page it should appear on is a candidate to check individually, turning N requests into N/20; (2) **HTTP conditional requests** — if AO3 honours `ETag` / `If-Modified-Since` on work pages, a 304 costs almost nothing, so verify that empirically before assuming it; (3) **event-driven checks** — a work is worth checking when the user acts on it (opens it, queues it, taps Refresh), which spreads the cost across real intent instead of a timer; (4) **a much longer interval** than the sweep's 7 days for anything unattended, plus a hard daily request budget shared with the rest of the app. Whatever the mechanism, the rule the sweep already follows should hold: serial, spaced, skip-recent, oldest-first, capped, cancellable, and never silent about what it did not do. | unassigned | — | 🅿️ IDEA | Do not make T-170 automatic on a timer without one of the above. Verify (2) with a real `curl -I` against a work page before building anything on it. |
| T-170 | **Library-wide availability sweep — manual, deliberately slow.** Closes the gap T-160 left: import checks the work it just added, but everything already in the Library stayed unchecked, so **Last Copy** only ever appeared for works something happened to touch. `WorkAvailabilitySweep` walks the library and asks AO3 about each work, surfaced as **Settings → Preservation → Check Availability…** with a sheet that states the cost *before* sending anything. **Never automatic**, at the owner's instruction. **Politeness, since that is the whole design constraint:** `AO3RequestCoordinator` bounds concurrency (3) but does **not** rate-limit, so a sweep left to inherit it would fire a sustained burst — this instead runs **serially with a 1.5s gap** (≈0.7 req/s, slower than a person clicking through pages), skips works checked in the last **7 days**, goes **oldest-checked first** so repeat runs cover a large library rather than re-walking the same prefix, caps a run at **150** works, and is cancellable between requests with completed work kept. Non-AO3 works are never contacted — there is no native path to those sites — and are *counted* so the summary explains why its number is smaller than the library. An inconclusive check (a timeout) counts as neither outcome, so a sweep cannot report a clean bill of health it did not earn. | Claude Opus 5 | `claude/non-epub-pdf-distribution-ab6eba` | ✅ DONE (unverified against live AO3) | 8 tests, mostly *politeness* assertions — what it skips, what it caps, that cancellation keeps its results — with the network stubbed through an injected `verify`. **Needs a human:** one real run against a live AO3 session, watching for 429s or Retry-After. | T-164 | **PDF layout: replace the hand-rolled heuristics with MuPDF — spike done, integration next.** Owner pushed back that this was being hand-rolled and that someone had surely solved it; they were right, and the evidence is five paragraph defects in one day, all from the same root cause: `PDFPage.string` + `characterBounds` is a *text* API, not a *layout* API — no guaranteed reading order, and frequently degenerate glyph boxes (one body line reported `maxX` **below** its own `minX`). The fifth defect silently relocated a clause into the wrong paragraph, which for a preservation app is the worst failure mode there is. **Proven, not assumed:** MuPDF cross-compiles for iOS device + simulator + macOS on the first attempt (`make libs` with an SDK-pointed `CC`, no patches), and `mutool draw -F stext` on page 14 of the owner's PDF — the page that produced defect 5 — returns the correct paragraph blocks in the correct order with the missing clause intact, where the hand-rolled path glued two lines together and moved a third elsewhere. Recipe captured in [`Scripts/build-mupdf.sh`](Scripts/build-mupdf.sh) incl. two build findings: each slice needs a **pristine tree** (`git clean -xfd`, because some third-party objects are written outside `OUT` and a HarfBuzz object kept its MACOS platform tag inside the simulator archive, which `-create-xcframework` rejects), and the two archives must be merged per slice with `libtool`. Full rationale, the calibre comparison, and the licence obligations in [`docs/PDF_ENGINE_MUPDF.md`](docs/PDF_ENGINE_MUPDF.md). **⚠️ Licence is an owner decision:** MuPDF is AGPL-3. GPL-3 §13 permits the combination, so it is legal here, but the combined work carries AGPL terms incl. a source offer — which means README/LICENSE wording and a ThirdPartyNotices entry (invariant 9 fails without it, by design). Owner approved vendoring it. | Claude Opus 5 | `claude/non-epub-pdf-distribution-ab6eba` | ✋ HANDOFF (spike done, not integrated) | **Next step:** add the xcframework to the project with a module map, write `MuPDFStructuredText` returning `[[String]]` per page in the shape `pageBlocks(of:)` already consumes, then delete `visualLines`, `runsSharingABaseline`, `blocks(from:)` and `reflowed` (~250 lines, the source of all five defects). Bump the converter to **v7** so every already-imported PDF offers Rebuild from Original — the versioning paying for itself. Keep PDFKit for `documentAttributes`; no reason to change two things at once. **Size, measured after the owner objected to 190 MB:** the size flags in the build script (drop bundled CJK/Noto fonts, drop XPS/SVG/HTML/EPUB/JS/OCR handlers) take `libmupdf.a` from **54 MB to 6.4 MB**, a merged slice to ~16 MB, and a **dead-stripped executable that opens a PDF and walks its structured text to 6.1 MB** — so the real app cost is ~6 MB, not 190. Extraction output is byte-identical after stripping (verified on the reference PDF): the dropped fonts only matter for *rendering*, while extraction reads the PDF's own encoding/ToUnicode. Nothing smaller solves it — PDFium and poppler give per-character positions or positioned HTML that still needs the failed heuristics, and Vision OCR would re-transcribe text we already have exactly. | T-157 | **PDF → EPUB conversion (the PDF half of T-153), with OCR for scans.** Owner overruled deferring PDF ("PDF converters exist out there") and was right. Deliberately *reflowable*, not fixed-layout: a PDF is page-shaped and Kudos is a reflowable reader, so this recovers prose and lets the reader lay it out. Lossy — acceptable only because T-152 archives the original PDF beside the EPUB. **Pipeline:** PDFKit text layer → Vision `VNRecognizeTextRequest` when the layer is missing or under 200 chars (a scan) → running headers/footers and bare page numbers dropped by cross-page frequency → paragraphs rebuilt → chapters from the PDF's own `outlineRoot` when present (calibre and Word both write one), else the shared plain-text heading rules. Metadata from `documentAttributes`, with Keywords → `dc:subject` because calibre writes a fic's AO3 tags there. **The load-bearing discovery:** a PDF records *no* paragraph breaks — PDFKit's extracted text drops blank lines entirely, verified with a throwaway probe rather than assumed. Boundaries are inferred from the ragged right edge: a line under 85% of the **90th-percentile** line width ends its paragraph (median is dragged down by the very short lines being detected; max would be inflated by one long URL). Chapter headings always break, or "Chapter 2" glues to the next sentence and that sentence becomes the chapter title. Rejected: "ends with .!? ⇒ paragraph end", which gives dialogue-heavy fic one paragraph per sentence. **Known-weak inputs:** poetry/lyrics, multi-column layouts (PDFKit interleaves columns), tables. **Researched and rejected as unembeddable on iOS:** calibre `ebook-convert` (Python), poppler `pdftohtml` (desktop C++ + fontconfig/freetype), `pdf2htmlEX` (emits fixed-layout EPUB, defeating the point). No new dependency — PDFKit and Vision are system frameworks, so invariant 9 (every Package.resolved pin needs a ThirdPartyNotices entry) is untouched. | Claude Opus 5 | `claude/non-epub-pdf-distribution-ab6eba` | ✅ DONE (OCR success path unverified) | 10 new tests; OCR capped at 60 pages with the drop logged, never silently truncated. **Needs a human:** a real scanned-prose PDF on device — a unit test cannot promise Vision will read a synthesized page image, so the test asserts the OCR path runs and then fails honestly on an unreadable scan. Also worth trying a calibre-exported fic PDF, the most likely real input. Remaining in T-153: DOCX via mammoth.js in JavaScriptCore, and RTF. |

| T-153 | **HIG Wave 10 (was filed as T-139; renumbered to T-153 at merge, see the note row) — Reader (UI-9 + BUG-7)** — the plan's own highest-risk, "sequential, last" wave, run here in parallel with Waves 6-9 per owner instruction (worktree cut from local `hig-review` tip `45dc317`, which is ahead of `origin/hig-review`). Closes UI-9, BUG-7, UI-2/UI-5/UI-3/UI-4's reader items, and the 3 folded reader-correctness findings (A7-F3/A7-F5/A7-F8) from `docs/audits/Fixes_and_HIG_Implementation_Plan.md`. | Claude | `claude/wave-10-implementation-d4eb95` | ✅ DONE, merge gated on owner macOS/iOS smoke | **UI-9 (platform-split code health).** `ReaderController.swift`/`ReaderView.swift` are both file-level `#if os(macOS)`-gated (iOS uses Readium) so every nested `#if os(iOS)` block inside them was unreachable dead code — removed (chrome tap/scroll gestures, `chromeVisible`/`isPhone` state, the dead `readerSheet` computed property + its `#if os(iOS)`-only toolbar/sheet chain, the `#if !os(macOS)` `navigationBarTitleDisplayMode`). `.presentationContentInteraction(.scrolls)` existed only in that dead copy — ported to the real iOS sheet in `ReadiumReaderView.swift`'s `readerSheet`. `ReaderTextStyle.letterSpacingRange` (-0.03…0.12, `ReaderStyle.swift`) rendered unclamped on macOS's CSS path (`ReaderStylesheet.css`) while `ReadiumReaderStyleMapper.preferences` clamps to ≥0 on iOS; `ReaderStylesheet.css` now clamps the same way so a shared persisted `readerLetterSpacing` can't render differently cross-platform (currently dormant in practice — no UI on either platform can set it negative today; this closes the gap for a `.kudosbackup`-restored or hand-edited value). **BUG-7 (P2, live-reproduced macOS bug).** The legacy reader's `.ignoresSafeArea()` call (`ReaderView.swift`) discarded `NavigationSplitView`'s overlay-sidebar leading safe-area inset along with the notch/home-indicator insets its own comment cited — both iOS-only concerns (this file has no tap-to-hide chrome; macOS's chapter controls stay docked unconditionally). Removed; reader content now respects the standard safe area like every other macOS detail-column screen. **Could not visually re-confirm this session** (no interactive macOS session) — owner should re-run the exact repro ("The Queen's Mercy" with the sidebar open). **UI-2.** `CustomizeThemeView`'s 4 sliders were VoiceOver-anonymous (raw % contradicting the visible "1.45"-style readout) — added `.accessibilityLabel`/`.accessibilityValue` matching `TextSizeSlider`'s existing, already-correct pattern; theme swatch selection was ring-color-only — added `.isSelected` trait + a non-color checkmark badge. **UI-5.** Reader-chrome fade (`ReadiumReaderView`) and `CustomizeThemeView`'s Customize-toggle/Reset-Theme animations were ungated — now use the shared `UIComponents/ReduceMotion.swift` helpers (`.animation(unlessReduced:value:)` / `withAnimationUnlessReduced`). **UI-3/UI-4.** The Readium progress pill's `.frame(height: 40)` clipped scaling `.footnote` text at large Dynamic Type — changed to `.frame(minHeight: 40)`. **A7-F3 (macOS controller/WebView retention).** `ReaderController`'s callback closures captured `ReaderView`'s `@State`, which owned the controller, with nothing ever clearing them — new idempotent `ReaderController.teardown()` nils every callback + stops loading, called from `.onDisappear`. **Adversarial review caught a real gap in the first version:** it also cleared `navigationDelegate`/removed the `"reader"` script message handler, but `wireController()` (`.onAppear`) never restores either — `navigationDelegate` is a `weak` WKWebView property (not actually part of the retain cycle) and clearing the handler would have silently, permanently broken the JS↔host bridge (layout injection, external-link routing) if the same controller instance ever became visible again without deallocating. Fixed: `teardown()` now only nils the 6 callbacks + `stopLoading()`. Also added a minimal `WebView.dismantleNSView` (`WebBrowser.swift`, `stopLoading()` only) to the shared wrapper used by both the reader and Browse's `BrowserModel` — safe for Browse's long-lived tab-persistent web view since it's a no-op unless something's actively loading. **A7-F5 (cross-spine links didn't update `currentIndex`).** A cross-chapter note link (e.g. `chapter2.xhtml#note` tapped from chapter 1) navigated the WKWebView directly, bypassing `currentIndex`/`work.lastSpineIndex`/the progress pill/TOC/chapter-aware comments scope, which stayed pinned to the old chapter. New `ReaderController.onCrossSpineNavigation` callback + `isCrossSpineNavigation(to:)` (compares `URL.path`, which excludes the fragment, so same-document anchor jumps still navigate in place unmodified) intercepts in `decidePolicyFor`; `ReaderView.wireController()`'s closure resolves the target against `document.spineURLs` and sets `currentIndex` (no explicit capture list, so it reads live state — matching every other callback in the same function), re-entering the existing `onChange(of: currentIndex)` → `loadCurrentChapter()` path. Scroll-to-the-exact-anchor inside the target chapter isn't restored — matches the finding's own "smallest safe correction" scope. **A7-F8 (iOS chapter sheet dropped nested TOC children).** `ReadiumReaderView.buildSections` only iterated `toc`'s top-level `Link`s; a Part→Chapter TOC hierarchy (`Link.children`, confirmed against the vendored Readium 3.9.0 source) left child chapters with no TOC entry, so they classified `.other` and the chapter sheet's `.filter { $0.kind != .other }` hid them. New `flattenTOC` recursively flattens depth-first (document order) before spine matching; deliberately adds no new dedup logic, relying on `ReaderSectionBuilder.build`'s own existing spine-indexed dictionary (verified directly against `Reading/ReaderSection.swift` — it already produces exactly one section per spine index regardless of duplicate/nested TOC entries pointing at the same file). `buildSections` bumped from `private` to internal so tests can call it directly. **Tests (7 new):** `ReadiumReaderTests` — nested TOC flattens into 3 sections none classified `.other` (would fail without the fix, since the un-flattened children fall to `.other` and get filtered from the chapter sheet), 3-level nesting preserves document order, existing flat-TOC shape unchanged (regression guard); `ReaderThemeTests` — negative letter-spacing emits no CSS rule, zero emits none, positive still renders. `ReaderController`'s macOS-only pieces (`teardown()`, `isCrossSpineNavigation`, the `.ignoresSafeArea()` removal) have no automated coverage — this project's test suite runs iOS-only (confirmed via `Scripts/test.sh`/`verify.sh`), matching the existing project-wide convention for WKWebView/macOS-legacy-reader logic. **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN — invariants, lint (pre-existing warnings only; `ReadiumReaderView.swift`'s `file_length` warning already existed at 750/700 lines on the base commit, confirmed by linting that exact revision in isolation before attributing it), **628 tests / 58 suites**, iOS build, macOS build, whitespace. **Adversarial review** (6-dimension parallel `Workflow` pass, each finding independently re-verified by a second agent against current source): 1 confirmed real — the `teardown()`/`wireController()` asymmetry above (fixed, then re-verified green with a second full `Scripts/verify.sh` run). The verifying agent's own follow-up traced the app's current macOS navigation structure and couldn't find an actually-reachable trigger for it today (tab switches/nav pushes appear to fully recreate `ReaderView`'s `@State`), so this was a latent correctness gap and a false claim in the original comment rather than an active bug — still worth closing, now closed. **Explicitly not implemented (out of this wave's scope per the plan):** T-98's scrolled-hang fix (separate, unmerged `reader-scrolled-hang-diagnosis` branch, owner-smoke-gated) and its 3 "accepted, no action" minor findings (T98-F4/F5/F6, `docs/SCROLLED_READER_HANG_FIX_ADVERSARIAL_REVIEW.md`) — the plan's own Wave 10 dependency note ("owner smoke on T-98") is a merge prerequisite, not something to reimplement here. **Review round (`docs/audits/WAVE_10_REVIEW_FIX_PROMPT.md`, 8 findings, all addressed 2026-07-25):** **W10-1 (blocker) — fixed.** `WebView.dismantleNSView` was added to the *shared* wrapper, which has 4 users (`BrowseView` `model.webView`, `AO3LoginView` `auth.loginWebView`, `ContentView`'s launch prewarm, and the reader) — SwiftUI dismantles a representable whenever it leaves the hierarchy, which includes a macOS tab switch *and* this reader's own `isLoading` skeleton swap, so the blanket `stopLoading()` would kill in-flight loads it never owned (my TASKS.md claim that it was "a no-op unless something's actively loading" had the logic exactly backwards — that *is* the damaging case). Deleted outright; `ReaderController.teardown()` already calls `stopLoading()` for the only case A7-F3 is about. Left a doc comment on `WebView` explaining why it must not have a dismantle hook, so it doesn't get re-added. **W10-2 (blocker) — stale, and the real half fixed.** The reported `ReadiumReaderView.swift` conflict no longer reproduces: Wave 8 reverted its own pill change in `9402456` (its tree now carries the original `.frame(height: 40)` and doesn't touch `progressPill`), so `git merge-tree` against `claude/wave-8-parallel-abc2f4` now conflicts only on `TASKS.md`. That makes Wave 10 sole owner, so I fixed the half the reviewer correctly noted *neither* wave addressed: `.lineLimit(1)` with no width constraint let the capsule grow **horizontally** past the safe area (`minHeight` only ever bought line-height). `bottomBar` now applies `.padding(.horizontal, 16)` *inside* its `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity)` so the pill receives a bounded proposal (16pt/edge = this app's established screen margin, same as T-137's clamp), plus `.minimumScaleFactor(0.75)` so the longest reading shrinks before truncating. **W10-3 — fixed, with one correction to the finding.** The behavior change is real: `ReaderSectionBuilder.build` is last-write-wins (`Reading/ReaderSection.swift`), so depth-first flattening let a child overwrite its parent when a grouping node shares its first child's href. `buildSections` now claims each spine index for the *outermost* entry (`claimedSpineIndices`), preserving exactly the title that won before flattening — so A7-F8 only *adds* the previously-missing children. `build`'s own last-wins contract is shared with the macOS pipeline and deliberately untouched; the "already resolved correctly" claim is gone from the comment. **The review's stated mechanism is wrong, though:** `.other` is assigned only when a spine item has *no* title at all, so a Part *with* a TOC entry was never `.other` and could not flip that way. The numbering shift is real but arrives via a parent/child **classification** flip (an `Afterword`/`Preface` parent losing to a chapter child). Proved both empirically by temporarily reverting the dedup and running the suite: without it, `partSharingItsFirstChildsHrefKeepsTheOutermostTitle` fails `["Chapter 1", "Chapter 2"] == ["Part One", "Chapter 2"]` and `classificationDoesNotFlipWhenAParentSharesAChildsHref` fails on both `[.chapter, .chapter] == [.chapter, .afterword]` **and `storyChapterCount → 2` vs `1`** — the off-by-one, reproduced. **W10-4 — fixed.** The host callback ran inside the `if` condition, i.e. before `decisionHandler`, and its body sets `currentIndex` → `loadCurrentChapter()` → `loadFileURL`. Split into a **pure** `recognizesSpineURL` query and an `onCrossSpineNavigation` action, so `decisionHandler(.cancel)` is final before anything can start a load. Also took both smaller notes: the `file` scheme check is now lowercased like the http/https one, and both sides of the path comparison go through a new shared `ReaderController.fileKey` (`standardizedFileURL.resolvingSymlinksInPath()`) so macOS's `/var` vs `/private/var` can't make a real cross-chapter link silently fall through to `.allow`. **W10-5 — fixed.** Narrowed `letterSpacingRange` to `0.0 ... 0.12` (it was still advertising a negative half all three consumers discard), deleted `CustomizeThemeView`'s now-redundant `supportedLetterSpacingRange` derivation in favour of the shared constant, and kept the renderer clamp with its one remaining job spelled out: `KudosBackupSettings` restores `readerLetterSpacing` as a raw `Double` with no range validation, so a legacy/hand-edited backup can still carry a negative. +1 test asserting the range's lower bound is 0. **W10-6 — fixed.** The badge no longer `.offset`s outside its frame (it lays out normally inside the swatch's bounds, so it can't be clipped by the row or spill onto a neighbour) and the hardcoded `.background(Circle().fill(.white))` is gone — palette rendering already fills the badge's own circle with the accent colour, so the white ring that read badly in Dark/OLED was never needed. Added 4pt around the swatch so the badge doesn't crowd its neighbour. **W10-7 — documented, not unified.** Added a comment explaining why the `Binding` case keeps its ternary: both shared `ReduceMotion` helpers are shaped for the other two forms (a `withAnimation` call site, a `.animation(_:value:)` modifier), and SwiftUI's own `Binding.animation(_:)` already takes an optional, so passing `nil` *is* the documented opt-out. Deliberately did not add a `Binding` overload to the Wave-1 shared file — it would still need `reduceMotion` threaded manually (a `Binding` can't read the environment), so it'd be no clearer than the ternary while widening the shared-file surface the parallel waves are already colliding on. **W10-8 — cannot be closed here; unchanged.** Both gates remain open and are the owner's: see "Remaining before merge" below. **Re-verified after the review round:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN — **631 tests / 58 suites** (628 + 3 new), iOS + macOS builds, lint (the one warning in a touched file is `ReadiumReaderView.swift`'s pre-existing `file_length`, 750→759 against a 700 threshold it already exceeded at the base commit), whitespace clean. **Remaining before merge (per the plan's own Wave 10 human-review gate):** owner iOS *and* macOS reader smoke — BUG-7's sidebar repro specifically, A7-F5's cross-chapter-note-link repro (now also worth watching the console for WebKit completion-handler warnings, per W10-4), A7-F3's open/close-×10 dealloc check **and** its navigate-away-and-back reappear path, A7-F8 on a real nested-TOC EPUB (ideally one with a shared Part/first-chapter href), **Browse tab-switch-during-load on macOS** (W10-1's repro — should now pass, and would have failed before this round), VoiceOver pass on the 4 `CustomizeThemeView` sliders + theme swatches, Reduce Motion check on the chrome fade, Dynamic Type check on the progress pill, plus the still-pending T-98 owner smoke this wave's own gate depends on. |
| T-148 | **HIG Wave 9 — Timing/navigation correctness (UI-8).** _(Includes the adversarial-review fix round for `docs/audits/WAVE_9_REVIEW_FIX_PROMPT.md` W9-1…W9-6.)_ ⚠️ **ID note:** this wave's commits are all messaged `T-139` — one of the four concurrent claims on that number described in the ⚠️ NOTE row below. Renumbered to **T-148** at merge, per that row's own instruction to renumber Wave 9/10 "from T-148 upward" (Wave 8 became T-147 the same way). Commit subjects were deliberately **not** rewritten, since they were already pushed. **This row is authoritative — in `git log`, `T-139` on `claude/wave-9-implementation-270b45` means this task.** **Net result: only 2 of UI-8's 4 magic-number sleeps were genuinely convertible to completion signals — not 3.** The review's W9-1 was correct and is the headline correction: `.alert` has **no** `onDismiss` parameter and SwiftUI provides no other "this alert is gone" event, and `onChange` on the alert's own `isPresented` binding is **not** a substitute — SwiftUI clears that binding to *begin* the dismissal, so the handler runs ~a frame after the tap with the alert still animating. My first pass mistook that state transition for a completion signal, which didn't convert the 350ms wait in `AuthorProfileView`'s "Log in to AO3" → login-sheet chain, it **deleted** it — reinstating verbatim the condition its own comment named ("Simultaneous alert-dismiss + sheet-present can drop the sheet on some iOS versions"), whose failure mode is a signed-out user tapping Subscribe/Mute/Block, tapping "Log In", and getting nothing, in a re-triggerable loop that's easy to miss in a smoke test. **Reverted to the tested 350ms sleep** and documented at the call site *why* it is not convertible, so the next reader doesn't re-attempt the same swap — same reasoning already applied to the 700ms case below, now applied consistently to both. Original convertible work (stands, and the review endorsed its call-site tracing): **`CommentsView.openAuthor`** (byline tap while `isModal`) and **`CommentComposerSheet.openParentAuthor`** (reply-quote author tap) both used to `dismiss()` then guess 350ms before `router.openAuthorProfile(route)` — "long enough for the sheet to finish animating away." Added `AppRouter.requestAuthorProfileAfterDismiss(_:)`/`openPendingAuthorProfileAfterDismiss()`: callers queue the route and `dismiss()` immediately; whichever modal presenter's own `onDismiss` fires *last* in the current chain calls `openPendingAuthorProfileAfterDismiss()` — a no-op if nothing's queued, so it's safe to wire at every presenter without knowing which one is outermost for a given call site. Wired at 3 presenters: `CommentsSheetModifier`'s `.sheet` (macOS + iOS) and `.fullScreenCover` (iOS expand mode) — covers `openAuthor` directly, and `openParentAuthor` when the enclosing `CommentsView` is modal; and the composer's own `.sheet(isPresented: composerBinding)` — covers `openParentAuthor` when the enclosing `CommentsView` is pushed (non-modal), guarded `if !isModal` so it doesn't fire early and bury the push under a still-dismissing outer sheet (the exact bug the original sleep guarded against). **Review fixes beyond W9-1.** **W9-2 (real bug, confirmed):** deleting the old `Task` blocks also deleted the `isOpeningModalAuthorProfile = false` / `isOpeningParentAuthor = false` resets inside them, leaving both re-entrancy flags **write-only** — latent today (every `isModal: true` site tears the view down, so fresh `@State` masks it) but a permanent silent latch, i.e. "tapping the author does nothing," for any future presenter that doesn't. **W9-3 (real bug, confirmed):** the new router queue had no overwrite protection, so a second tap from a *different* view silently replaced the first (the old per-view booleans couldn't see each other), and a stranded route could later fire on an unrelated dismissal. Fixed both together by deleting both local `@State` flags and making the router's single queued route *be* the guard: `requestAuthorProfileAfterDismiss` is now `@discardableResult -> Bool` that refuses to overwrite (**first tap wins**, matching the old semantics) and callers `guard` on it before dismissing — one piece of shared state instead of two per-view booleans that can disagree, and it cannot latch because draining clears it. Added `cancelPendingAuthorProfileAfterDismiss()`, called from `CommentsSheetModifier` on the **rising** edge of `isPresented` (provably cannot race a drain — queuing only ever happens on the way *out* of a presentation), so a strand can't outlive one Comments presentation. The review's suggested per-presenter *token* scoping was deliberately not built: the queuing view (`CommentsView`/the composer) and the draining view (`CommentsSheetModifier`) are different `View` structs, so a token minted by the former can't reach the latter's `onDismiss` — the presenter contract is instead documented explicitly on the router API (over-hooking is safe; **under**-hooking is the hazard). **W9-4 (plausible):** no code change — the 700ms window now opens when `onDismiss` fires rather than 350ms after `dismiss()`; folded into the outstanding smoke checklist rather than blind-retuned. **W9-5:** Branch column corrected (was `hig-review`). **W9-6:** added 4 `AppRouterTests` covering the queue state machine — queue→drain pushes once, a second drain no-ops, second-tap-mid-dismiss is refused without overwriting, draining releases the guard for later taps, and cancel drops a stranded route; `secondTapMidDismissIsRefused…` is the one that would have caught W9-3(b). **Owner-found navigation bug, fixed in-wave (pre-existing, not a Wave 9 regression).** Owner testing of the pre-merge checklist hit: in **pushed** (non-modal) Comments, the composer's reply-quote author tap dismissed the composer and saved the draft but **no profile appeared**; and a plain comment-byline tap in the same pushed Comments *did* push the profile but **Back skipped Comments entirely**, landing on Work Detail's Discussion tab. One root cause: `WorkDetailSections` pushed Comments with a **destination-based** `NavigationLink { CommentsView(…) }`, which puts the pushed view *outside* the enclosing stack's `NavigationPath` — so `AO3AuthorNavigationModifier`'s `path.append(route)` didn't stack on top of Comments, it **discarded** it. Two symptoms, same cause: the discard alone explains the broken Back; when the append additionally raced the dismissing composer sheet, the whole push was dropped. Not a Wave 9 regression — the pre-wave code ended its 350ms sleep with the identical `router.openAuthorProfile(route)` call, so both symptoms predate it; Wave 9's checklist merely surfaced them. **Fix:** added `AO3CommentsRoute` (`Hashable`; `AO3CommentsWorkContext` already conformed) and registered `.navigationDestination(for: AO3CommentsRoute.self)` inside `AO3AuthorNavigationModifier` — the same "declare it once, every root stack gets it" spot the author and series destinations already use, so all 5 roots (`Home`/`Library`/`Search`/`Browse`/`Account`) are covered without duplication — then converted all 3 WorkDetail links (All Comments, Chapter Comments, Write a Comment) to `NavigationLink(value:)`. Comments now lives in the path, so the author push is a plain append on top of it. The Account **inbox** route was already value-based (`AccountInboxThreadDestination`) and needed no change; the **modal** `commentsSheet` paths are unaffected (they dismiss the sheet entirely rather than appending under it), which is why the modal byline tap always worked. **`AppRouter`'s 700ms `cardNavigationSuppressed` window left untouched, deliberately:** investigated first — it's structurally a different problem from the other 3 (a same-touch dual-gesture-recognizer race between a List row's background `NavigationLink` and a `highPriorityGesture` author-tap, not a modal dismiss-then-push sequence), and SwiftUI exposes no completion event for "this touch's gesture recognizers have finished resolving." The existing design already does the deterministic part correctly (destination views self-check `shouldSuppressCardNavigation` + a `<0.5s` `appearedAt` guard rather than trusting the timer alone); 700ms is just how long that checking window stays open, tuned by prior live device testing (see its inline comment). Changing the number without equivalent on-device verification risked reintroducing the exact regression it was tuned to prevent — out of proportion for a "Small–Medium" wave per the plan. | Claude | claude/wave-9-implementation-270b45 | ✅ DONE (runtime-verified on iOS; 3 checks unreachable/unrun — listed below) | **Verified:** lint (`Scripts/lint.sh`) **0 errors**, only pre-existing warnings; iOS **626 tests / 58 suites** `** TEST SUCCEEDED **` (**+4** over the 622 baseline — the new `AppRouterTests` queue cases, asserting the exact states W9-2/W9-3 got wrong); iOS **and** macOS Debug builds both `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`; `git status` clean (no pbxproj churn). **Runtime-verified live** (dedicated iPhone 17 Pro Max / iOS 26.5, deliberately *not* the shared iPhone 17 a concurrent Wave-8 agent was holding with a live orientation log-stream — headless MCP input, so no screen-focus contention; real signed-out AO3 session, Browse ▸ Harry Potter ▸ "When the Stars Forgot Your Name", which had one real comment from registered user `Mandu_pandu`). **Passed — (b) modal-sheet byline tap:** the profile pushed *on top* of the stack with a working Back, not buried behind a still-dismissing sheet — this is the direct proof that `CommentsSheetModifier`'s `.sheet(onDismiss:)` actually fires and drains the queue in the real app, which is the entire mechanism this wave rests on. **Passed — (b) latch check:** re-opened Comments and tapped the byline a second time; it pushed again. This is the check that matters most for the *review fix specifically*, because moving the guard from per-view `@State` (self-healing on teardown) to app-lifetime router state introduced a brand-new latch risk that view teardown would NOT mask — confirmed absent. **Passed — (e) expand handoff:** Expand promoted the sheet to a `fullScreenCover` normally, proving the drain call added into that same `onDismiss` no-ops during the handoff instead of hijacking it; then a byline tap from full-screen mode pushed the profile, exercising the `.fullScreenCover(onDismiss:)` hook that did not exist before this wave. **Passed — (e) strand check (W9-3a):** opening Comments and closing it with X (nothing queued) returned to the list with **no** phantom profile push. **(a) is NOT REACHABLE as the review described it** — investigated rather than skipped: `AO3AuthorHero` only renders Subscribe/Mute/Block `if let form = header.subscriptionForm` / `if let muteAction`, and AO3 only exposes those forms to a signed-in session, so a signed-out user has no button to tap and `subscriptionTapped`'s `guard auth.isLoggedIn` branch can only be hit in the narrow race where the session drops *after* a signed-in render (confirmed in code and on-screen — the signed-out profile's ⋯ menu offers only Open on AO3 / Share Profile). | T-152 | **Import/merge UX: alert-style confirmation, a real "what changed" summary, and a progress indicator (owner-requested).** Three asks, one of which turned out to be a bug rather than a missing feature. **(a) The post-merge confirmation never appeared — root cause was three stacked `.alert` modifiers.** `SettingsView`'s modifier chain carried `.alert(item: $backupNotice)`, `.alert(item: $epubNotice)` and (after T-151) the import-confirmation alert all on the *same* view. SwiftUI honours only one `.alert` per view and silently drops the rest, so `backupNotice` — which already contained a "Backup Imported / Merged N records…" message — had **never** presented for either export or import; both operations completed looking like they had done nothing. Replaced all of them with a single `.alert(item: activeAlertBinding)` driven by a new `SettingsAlert` enum (`.notice` / `.confirmImport`), with a projecting `Binding` so every existing call site keeps setting `backupNotice`/`epubNotice`/`showImportConfirmation` unchanged. Also added `KudosBackupRestoreSummary.changeMessage` (sibling to the existing `conflictMessage`) listing what actually landed, one item per line, including zero counts so "none of this kind was in the backup" reads differently from "these were skipped"; the alert now shows "Merged into your library: • 7 Library records • 0 saved links • 0 custom fonts" plus any conflict caveats. **(b) Import confirmation is now an `.alert`, not a `.confirmationDialog`.** Presented from a List row on iPhone the dialog rendered as an anchored popover that *dropped its `.cancel` button entirely* (tap-outside was the only escape) — the owner correctly asked for a proper pop-up with Cancel. An alert renders both buttons explicitly and suits a decision that merges into the whole library. **(c) Progress indicator** — `isImportingBackup` state plus a linear `ProgressView` in the Backup section, and both rows now disable while either operation runs (previously Import stayed tappable during an export). Deliberately **indeterminate**: neither `writeArchive` nor `restore` reports per-record progress, and a bar inventing a percentage would misrepresent how far a merge had got. Also folded in: `restorePendingBackup` now takes the backup as a parameter and hops through a `Task { @MainActor }` before working, so the confirmation finishes dismissing and the progress row renders before the merge begins. | Claude | claude/wave-8-parallel-abc2f4 | ✅ DONE | **Verified live on iPhone 17 sim, full round trip:** Import Backup → picker → tapped the archive → **alert with both "Import and Merge" and "Cancel" rendered centred** → Import and Merge → **"Backup Imported — Merged into your library: • 7 Library records • 0 saved links • 0 custom fonts"** appeared, the first time any completion confirmation has ever shown. `Scripts/verify.sh` green. **Honest limits:** the progress bar is real but a 7-work merge finishes too fast to photograph, so it is code-verified rather than screenshot-verified at that size; and because `restore` runs synchronously on the main actor, on a very large library the bar will appear but may not animate during the blocking work — making it genuinely determinate (and animated) requires instrumenting `KudosBackupService.restore` to report progress and yield, which is a change to a critical persistence path and is **not** done here. Worth a follow-up task if large-library imports feel frozen. |
| T-151 | **`.kudosbackup` files were greyed out (unselectable) in the import picker — owner-reported; both backup formats were affected.** Root-caused empirically, not guessed: the app never *declared* its document type, so `UTType(filenameExtension: "kudosbackup", conformingTo: .zip)` minted a synthetic identifier encoding the assumed conformance (`dyn.ah62d46dzqm0gw23stmy1appbsmv0u4p0…`), while the system independently typed a real backup file from its extension alone (`dyn.ah62d4rv4ge8007per730e2pdrr41a`). Verified with a standalone `swiftc` probe against the actual exported file: `actual.conforms(to: .kudosBackup)` == **false**, so `fileImporter(allowedContentTypes:)` filtered out every backup. The legacy directory-package form was broken the same way and worse — a real legacy backup resolves to plain `public.folder`, matching neither the old `.kudosBackupLegacyPackage` (ext + `.package`, which matched *nothing*) nor anything else, so legacy import had been silently dead too. **Fix:** declared the type properly — `UTExportedTypeDeclarations` (`com.cidy02.Kudos.backup`, extension `kudosbackup`, conforming to `public.zip-archive`) injected into the generated Info.plist by the existing "Inject Info.plist keys" build phase, which exists for exactly this class of array-valued key that `GENERATE_INFOPLIST_FILE`'s `INFOPLIST_KEY_*` mechanism cannot express. `UTType.kudosBackup` now resolves that declared identifier, via a deliberately **non-trapping** lookup with an extension-derived fallback (unlike `UTType(exportedAs:)`, which aborts the app if the declaration is ever missing). Dropped `kudosBackupLegacyPackage` and allowed `.folder` for backup import instead, which is what legacy backups actually are. ⚠️ **Touches `project.pbxproj`** (per AGENTS.md, one agent at a time): the diff is **+28 lines, 0 deletions**, purely additive inside the existing script phase, with no cosmetic build churn. | Claude | claude/wave-8-parallel-abc2f4 | ✅ DONE | **Verified live end-to-end on iPhone 17 sim:** confirmed the declaration lands in the built `Kudos.app/Info.plist`; then in the app, Import Backup → **all three `.kudosbackup` files now render black with proper ZIP document icons and are selectable** (previously greyed out), and the legacy `KudosLibrary.kudosbackup` folder is selectable too. Completed the round trip: selected the archive exported by T-149 → confirmation read "This backup contains 7 Library records" (matching the 7 EPUBs `unzip -l` shows) → merged with no crash and no console exceptions. `Scripts/verify.sh` green. |

It was the **only** material background in the app, the same kind of outlier `BugReportView` was in T-132. Swapped it for `theme.appTheme.cardSurface`, the codebase's existing raised-surface token — whose `appElevatedBackground` doc comment literally names "list/form cells, **bars**, popovers" — which keeps the same one-step lift over the page background and still falls back to `secondarySystemGroupedBackground`/`controlBackgroundColor` on Light/Dark. Nothing is lost by dropping the material: this footer is a sibling *below* the `ScrollView` in a `VStack`, never an overlay, so no content ever scrolls behind it for a blur to reveal. Fixes `WelcomeView` too, since both share the scaffold. | T-149 | **Export Backup crashed the app the instant it was tapped (owner-reported, release blocker).** Tapping Settings ▸ Backup ▸ "Export Backup…" killed the app with `SIGABRT`. Root cause captured from a live console repro, not inferred: `*** Terminating app due to uncaught exception 'NSInvalidArgumentException', reason: '-[NSFileWrapper setPreferredFilename:] *** preferredFilename cannot be empty.'`, thrown under `FileImportExportBridge.presentExportPicker → FileExportOperation.writeTemporaryFilesIfNecessary() → AnyTransferable.newFileWrapper(contentType:)`. **Why:** T-145 replaced the old `FileDocument` exporter with the item-based `fileExporter(isPresented:item:contentTypes:defaultFilename:)` + a `Transferable` (`KudosBackupArchiveFile`). That `Transferable` declared a bare `FileRepresentation` with **no `suggestedFileName`**, and — verified empirically — the `defaultFilename:` argument does **not** feed this code path, so SwiftUI handed `NSFileWrapper` an empty preferred filename and Foundation raised. The export logic itself (plan, ZIP64 streaming writer, limits preflight) was never reached and was never at fault; the crash is purely in presenting the picker. Not caught by `verify.sh` because it is an ObjC exception raised inside SwiftUI's presentation machinery at runtime — there is no view-layer test that presents a `fileExporter`. **Fix, two parts:** (1) added `.suggestedFileName("Kudos Backup.kudosbackup")` to `KudosBackupArchiveFile.transferRepresentation` — this alone stops the crash, confirmed by isolating it as the only change and re-running the repro; (2) the picker then showed the raw temp name (`KudosBackupExport-ED8F…`), because this exporter takes the presented filename from the exported file's **URL**. So `exportBackup()` now writes to `tmp/KudosBackupExport-<UUID>/Kudos Backup <yyyy-MM-dd>.kudosbackup` — UUID moved into a wrapping *directory* so the file itself carries the human-readable name while concurrent/same-day exports still can't collide — `cleanUpBackupExportFile()` removes that directory rather than just the archive, and `defaultFilename:` is now derived from the archive's own URL so the two can never disagree. | Claude | claude/wave-8-parallel-abc2f4 | ✅ DONE | **Verified end-to-end on iPhone 17 sim, live:** reproduced the crash on the pre-fix build (twice, incl. one run with `--console-pty` attached to capture the exception text above), then on the fixed build tapped Export Backup → **save sheet presents** with "Save as: Kudos Backup 2026-07-25" → tapped Save → **no crash**, returned to Settings cleanly. Confirmed on disk afterwards: the archive landed in "On My iPhone" (`File Provider Storage/Kudos Backup 2026-07-25.kudosbackup`, 325 KB), `unzip -t` reports **"No errors detected"**, and it contains `manifest.json` + 7 `Works/*.epub` entries; **zero leftover `KudosBackupExport-*` temp directories**, so the new cleanup path works. Zero exceptions in the post-fix console log and no new crash report. `Scripts/verify.sh` green. **Worth a follow-up:** no regression test covers this (a `fileExporter` presentation can't be unit-tested here) — the guard is the `suggestedFileName` doc comment explaining that removing it re-crashes the app. |

| T-147 | **HIG Wave 8 (was filed as T-139; renumbered to T-147 at merge, see the note row) — Dynamic-Type structural (UI-4), remaining items after T-133–T-138.** The carousel cover-card growth/clamp/pin + compact-grid AX-collapse were already delivered by T-133–T-138; this closes the rest of UI-4's `docs/audits/Fixes_and_HIG_Implementation_Plan.md` Wave 8 scope: (1) `WorkDetailView` 4-tab segmented control → `.menu` picker fallback at accessibility sizes (native segmented caps label scaling instead of reflowing, mirrors the doc-comment `quickActionColumns` idiom); (2) Account Overview shortcut grid 3-col → 2-col reflow at AX (was shrinking text via `minimumScaleFactor` at a fixed 3-col); (3) `HomeSectionListView` "See all" grid fixed-2-col → responsive columns (widens on iPad/macOS, still 1-col at AX, tracks scaled card width); (4) `WorkDetailComponents` hero-fandoms `lineLimit(3)` → full wrap at AX (P3); ~~(5) Readium reader progress pill~~ — **dropped in the review round, see W8-2 below; Wave 10 owns the reader.** **Adversarial review caught a real regression before commit:** item (3)'s first pass used `CarouselCardMetrics.adaptiveCardColumns` unconditionally, which an independent layout-lens reviewer proved (exact arithmetic, verified by a second adversarial pass) collapses to **1 column at default text size on every 375pt-wide iPhone** (SE 2nd/3rd gen, 8/X/XS/11 Pro, 12/13 mini) — 2×164pt cards + 16pt gap = 344pt, 1pt over the 343pt available width, so `.adaptive(minimum:)` alone drops a column. Fixed by adding `CarouselCardMetrics.responsiveCardColumns(for:horizontalSizeClass:minimum:count:)` (`UIComponents/CarouselCardStyle.swift`) — fixed 2-up on **compact** width (iPhone, unconditionally 2 columns, never fewer), adaptive on **regular** width (iPad/macOS/landscape), 1-column at AX either way. The same reviewer flagged (refuted as out-of-diff-scope, but a valid parity note) that `ReadingQueueDetailView.compactGrid` — a structurally identical "see all" cover-card grid — still used the old fixed `compactCardColumns` and would visibly diverge from the fixed Home grid on iPad; folded that parity fix into this task too (added `cardSize`/`horizontalSizeClass`, swapped to `responsiveCardColumns`; kept it **non-private** per the T-136 memberwise-init gotcha, since `ReadingQueueDetailView` has no explicit `init` and is constructed via `ReadingQueueDetailView(queue:)` from `LibraryView.swift:148`). Ran in parallel with Wave 7 (Comments/Authors) on a separate worktree — file sets are disjoint. **── REVIEW ROUND (`docs/audits/WAVE_8_REVIEW_FIX_PROMPT.md`, reviewing `d620af3`) ──** An external review returned 2 blockers, 2 real bugs, 1 minor and 1 process finding; all 5 code findings are fixed. **W8-1 (blocker, confirmed against the code) — my `responsiveCardColumns` compact branch reintroduced the very UI-4 failure it was added to prevent.** Its compact-width path delegated to `compactCardColumns`, which returns fixed `GridItem(.flexible())` columns — and `.flexible()` splits the width evenly while *ignoring its children's intrinsic width*. But these cards pin their own outer width: `WorkSummaryCardSurface` renders at exactly `cardSize.width` (`.frame(width: cardSize.width - 24)` + `.padding(12)`, `HomeCards.swift:301`), which is `@ScaledMetric`-driven. So at `.xLarge` on a 375pt phone the card is ~183pt inside a 163.5pt column — ~20pt of overhang per card, ~59pt at `.xxxLarge`, i.e. the two columns visibly collide. My earlier reasoning (and the previous commit message) did the 344-vs-343pt arithmetic *only at the default text size* and stopped there; `isAccessibilitySize` doesn't fire until `accessibility1`, so every large-but-not-accessibility size in between was unguarded. T-137's clamp doesn't help — it bounds a *single* card against the screen, not two against a column. **Fix:** deleted both `responsiveCardColumns` and (now-unused) `compactCardColumns` and gave the surviving `adaptiveCardColumns` a documented 2pt `tolerance` subtracted from its `minimum`. `.adaptive` is the only variant that reacts to the card growing, and it does the container-width arithmetic itself, so no `GeometryReader`/size-class plumbing is needed — the `@Environment(\.horizontalSizeClass)` additions to both views were reverted. The tolerance exists solely for the opposite, sub-pixel case (two 164pt cards + 16pt gap need 344pt where the padded grid offers 343 — a 1pt miss that would otherwise cost a whole column); verified by arithmetic across the matrix that it flips **exactly one** cell (375pt @ default: 1→2 columns) and is inert at every other size/device, while every count now tracks real card width: 375pt and 390pt phones give 2 columns at default and 1 from `.xLarge` up (correct — two ~183pt cards genuinely cannot fit 343–358pt), iPad gives 4/4/3/3. Net −14 lines and **one** column rule now shared by all three cover-card grids (Home, Reading Queue, `LibraryEntityGridView`), which is the consistency the reviewer flagged as missing. **⚠️ Visible behaviour change for the owner to sanity-check:** the see-all grids now drop to a single column one notch above default text size, where they previously showed two overlapping cards. **W8-2 (blocker) — direct merge conflict with Wave 10 on the reader progress pill, with contradictory intent** (Wave 8 capped Dynamic Type at accessibility2; Wave 10 deliberately left growth unbounded). Reverted `ReadiumReaderView.swift` wholesale to the shared base: Wave 10 is the reader's owner (the plan makes it "sequential, last" with its own gate) and Wave 8's own scope line takes the pill only "*if not already handled*" — it was. The two branches now merge cleanly on code (`TASKS.md` still conflicts, see the collision note above). **This also drops Wave 8's answer to W8-4** (the pill's real overflow axis is horizontal — `.lineLimit(1)` with no width bound — which neither wave's change addressed); **handed to Wave 10 as an open item**, not silently lost. **W8-3 (real bug) — the `.menu` fallback leaked a developer-facing string.** The menu branch dropped `.labelsHidden()`, so `"Work Details Section"` — an accessibility/identity string, not product copy — became the visible leading label at accessibility sizes. Restored `.labelsHidden()` on both branches: it hides the label visually while VoiceOver still announces it, and a `.menu` picker still displays the selected tab as its value, so information density matches the segmented control. (My previous row asked the owner to eyeball this; the reviewer was right that it shouldn't ship on an aesthetic judgement call.) **W8-5 (minor) — fixed both doc comments** that cited `WorkDetailView.quickActionColumns`; it lives in `WorkDetailOverviewSections.swift:54`. **W8-6 (process) — still open, see below.** | Claude | claude/wave-8-parallel-abc2f4 | ✅ DONE (runtime gate run) | **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` **ALL GREEN**, run three times (initial, after the first grid fix, after the review round) — invariants, lint (**zero new warnings**; every remaining warning in a touched file is pre-existing and traced to the shared base, incl. `CarouselCardStyle.swift:57`'s `UIScreen.main` deprecation, which `git blame` puts at `45dc317`/T-137 and which appears in the pre-fix log too), iOS **`TEST SUCCEEDED`**, macOS Debug **`BUILD SUCCEEDED`**, whitespace clean. Merge-cleanliness re-checked with `git merge-tree` against `claude/wave-10-implementation-d4eb95`: **no code conflict** after the W8-2 revert (`TASKS.md` still conflicts — unavoidable, see the collision note). Earlier in-session **4-lens adversarial review** (API-correctness, VoiceOver/a11y, layout-regression, scope-completeness) caught the first-pass 375pt density regression; the external review then caught what that round missed (W8-1's `.xLarge` overflow) — the honest lesson is that **both** review passes reasoned about the default text size and only the second checked the sizes in between. **✅ THE MANDATORY RUNTIME AX GATE RAN** (`Fixes_and_HIG_Implementation_Plan.md:475`) — the owner granted Simulator control on request, so this is now observation, not arithmetic. iPhone 17 (390pt) / iOS 26.5, Sepia, real seeded library data, sweeping Dynamic Type live via `xcrun simctl ui booted content_size`. **(1) W8-1 — Home → "Reading Now" → See all → Compact grid, swept `.large` → `.xLarge` → `.xxxLarge` → `accessibility1`: 2, 2, 1, 1 columns, and — the thing that actually matters — NO card overhangs or overlaps its neighbour at any size.** W8-1 is fixed in observable fact, not just in theory. **Note: this pass falsified my own predicted table.** I had predicted 1 column from `.xLarge` up, reasoning that `@ScaledMetric(relativeTo: .body)` scales a 164pt base by the body point ratio (19/17 → ~183pt). It doesn't: `UIFontMetrics` compresses growth for large base values, so the real card is ~167pt at `.xLarge` and two still fit. The error was in the safe direction (more density retained than predicted, still no overlap) — but it was an error, and it is the second time in this task that arithmetic-only reasoning about this grid proved wrong. **Treat the numbers in the W8-1 note above as the model, and these screenshots as the truth.** **(2) W8-3 — Work Detail at `accessibility1`: the section control renders as a `.menu` row reading only "Overview ⇅" — no "Work Details Section" label — inside the themed card row, reading as hub chrome; opening it shows all four tabs (Overview ✓ / Tags / Discussion / Library) at full size with the selection checkmark.** Confirmed fixed and functional. **(3) Hero fandoms at `accessibility1`:** the 4-fandom list wraps to 4 full lines (previously clamped to 3), fully readable, with the stat row still rendering inside the hero card — no clipping, no overflow. **Not verified, and honestly out of reach here:** the **Account Overview shortcut grid** (3→2 reflow) — that grid only renders for a signed-in AO3 account, and I will not enter credentials; the **Reading Queue detail** compact grid (needs a queue containing works — the seeded data has none); and the **iPad regular-width widening** (would need a second simulator booted/installed; the `.adaptive` widening it relies on is pre-existing behaviour that `LibraryEntityGridView` already depended on, and the overlap risk — the part that was actually broken — is verified above). Those three remain owner checks. **(4) Reader pill is no longer this branch's** — verify under Wave 10, and carry W8-4 (horizontal overflow: `.lineLimit(1)`, no width bound) there. |

Nothing is lost by dropping the material: this footer is a sibling **below** the `ScrollView` in the scaffold's `VStack`, never an overlay, so no content ever scrolls behind it for the blur to reveal. | T-143 | **Fix F5/F6 (`docs/audits/Review_45dc317_Regression_Findings.md`) — superseded Inbox page-load could overwrite a newer page's state with a bogus pagination-failed error; new concurrent-request regression test.** `AO3InboxModel.load()`'s catch chain caught `CancellationError` but not `URLError(.cancelled)` — when `goToPage()` supersedes an in-flight page request (e.g. tapping "Next" twice before the first page answers), `launch()` cancels the prior `Task`, but a real cancelled `URLSession` request surfaces as `URLError(.cancelled)`, which fell through to the generic `catch` and wrote `phase = .paginationFailed(requestedPage:, …)` over whatever newer page had already loaded — `isCurrent(expected, auth)` only compares auth scope/generation, identical for two same-session page loads, so it never caught this. The exact same fix already existed once in this file, in the metadata-enrichment loop (`catch let error as URLError where error.code == .cancelled`) — `load()` just never got the matching clause. **Fix:** mirrored that precedent, one added `catch` case before the generic catch, returns silently instead of clobbering state. **Test-first (F6):** `AO3InboxPaginationFailureTests.swift` had 2 tests but both polled each `goToPage`/`retry` to completion before issuing the next, so only one `load()` was ever in flight — never exercised the overlapping-request path. Added `supersededPage2CancellationCannotOverwriteAnAlreadyLandedPage3`, using the `Signal`-gated `pageLoader` harness from `AO3InboxAccountTransitionTests.swift`: gates page 2, calls `goToPage(2)`, lets it block, calls `goToPage(3)` while 2 is still gated and lets it succeed, then releases page 2 so it throws `URLError(.cancelled)` and asserts the final state stays page-3 `.loaded`. **Explicitly confirmed RED before the fix** (failed with the exact bogus `.paginationFailed(requestedPage: 2, …)`, real `NSURLErrorDomain -999` under the hood) **and GREEN after** — independently re-run by the reviewer at both the parent and fix commits, confirming the red/green story is real, not vacuous. | Claude | hig-review | ✅ DONE | **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` **ALL GREEN** — invariants, lint (same 2 pre-existing `AO3InboxModel.swift` warnings, confirmed via stash-diff, nothing new), iOS **624 tests / 58 suites** (+1 over parent's 623) `** TEST SUCCEEDED **`, macOS Debug build `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, whitespace clean. Red-then-green independently re-confirmed by an adversarial reviewer, not just self-reported. |
| T-142 | **Fix F1/F2 (`docs/audits/Review_45dc317_Regression_Findings.md`) — compact-card grids overlapped at standard large text sizes, and 3 more hardcoded-2-column grids reproduced the original owner-reported bug.** **Root cause:** `CarouselCardStyle.swift` had two disagreeing Dynamic-Type mechanisms — `ScaledCarouselCardSize` (`@ScaledMetric`) scales a carousel card's rendered width continuously at *every* text-size step, but `compactCardColumns` only collapsed 2→1 columns at the five *accessibility* sizes. Between those regimes (ordinary large sizes like Extra Large/XXL/XXXL) a 2-column `LazyVGrid` still laid out narrow columns while the card inside pinned itself wider via `cardSize.width`, and `LazyVGrid` doesn't clip an oversized child, so the two cards in a row rendered overlapping (**F1**, reproducible at standard Settings ▸ Text Size, no accessibility toggle needed). Separately, 3 more compact grids never got Dynamic-Type-aware columns at all — `LibrarySectionListView.compactGrid` and `AccountComponents.swift`'s `AccountWorksCompactGrid`/`AccountBookmarksCompactGrid` (the latter two are Account ▸ My Works/Bookmarks/History/Subscriptions' *default* view, no toggle needed) — hardcoding `[GridItem(.flexible()), GridItem(.flexible())]`, reproducing the original character-by-character text-wrap bug T-133 only partially fixed (**F2**). **Fix:** converted all 5 compact-grid call sites (`HomeSectionListView.compactGrid`, `ReadingQueueDetailView.compactGrid`, `LibrarySectionListView.compactGrid`, `AccountWorksCompactGrid`, `AccountBookmarksCompactGrid`) to `CarouselCardMetrics.adaptiveCardColumns(for:minimum:spacing:)` — the same helper `LibraryEntityGridView` already used correctly — fed each view's own `ScaledCarouselCardSize().width` instead of a fixed count or the unscaled static default; .isSelected : [])`) directly to the existing row instead of forcing a controller-based consolidation the wave didn't call for. **UI-3 (`CommentsView.swift`) — pagination/sort controls below 44pt.** Applied the shared `.minimumHitTarget()` to the sort `Menu` trigger and both Previous/Next pagination buttons, matching this file's other controls. **UI-5 (ungated animations) — `CommentThreadRow.swift` + `CommentsView.swift` + `BrowseView.swift`.** Routed every previously-bare `withAnimation`/`.animation` call through the shared `UIComponents/ReduceMotion.swift` helpers (`withAnimationUnlessReduced`/`.animation(unlessReduced:value:)`, built in an earlier wave): the "Show less/Read more" toggle and `expandRepliesButton` (imperative form), the highlight fade (declarative form), `CommentsView`'s two focus-scroll `proxy.scrollTo` animations, and `BrowseView`'s toast show/hide — each file gained its own `@Environment(\.accessibilityReduceMotion)` where it didn't already have one. **CAA-7 (folded, `AO3Client+Comments.swift` + `CommentThreadRow.swift`) — deep-thread cutoff nodes collided as one SwiftUI identity.** AO3's depth-5+ thread cutoff (`<li class="comment">` with no id, no `role`, a single paragraph linking `/comments/<id>` reading "N more comments in this thread") was falling into the generic id-less "couldn't be read" tombstone path, whose fallback identity hashes the (always-empty, for this shape) `id` attribute — every cutoff on a page collided onto one negative id as SwiftUI row identity, and the count/continuation link were silently discarded. | T-138 | **Reading-progress/footer group pins to the card's true bottom regardless of how much metadata precedes it (owner: "the 'Reading' label and progress bar currently changes position based on how much metadata a card contains. pin it to the bottom of the card, with padding equivalent to the edge padding")** — `WorkCoverCard`/`AO3WorkCoverCard`'s `Spacer(minLength: 4)` (between the metadata stack and the trailing progress/footer group) could only ever add its 4pt minimum, never expand to fill the card's full height, because T-134's `.fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)` on `WorkSummaryCardSurface`'s content locked the whole VStack to its own natural content height — decoupling it from the taller `minHeight: cardSize.height` floor above it, so any extra proportional height landed *outside* the VStack's own bounds where the Spacer couldn't reach it. **Fix:** removed `.fixedSize(vertical:)` from `WorkSummaryCardSurface`. This is safe because T-134's *other* half — pinning content to an explicit, unambiguous `.frame(width:)` before measuring — was the actual fix for the original FlowLayout undershoot bug (giving `sizeThatFits` a consistent width across every SwiftUI negotiation pass); `.fixedSize` turned out to be unnecessary insurance once the width ambiguity was gone, and was actively the thing blocking this request. Without it, the content VStack (containing a `Spacer`) now naturally stretches to fill `.frame(minHeight:)`'s full resolved height exactly as a flexible view is supposed to, so the Spacer expands into all the "extra" proportional space and the progress/footer group lands flush against the card's true bottom edge — inheriting the same 12pt `.padding(12)` inset every other edge of the card already has, satisfying "padding equivalent to the edge padding" without any separate padding logic to get wrong. | Claude | hig-review | ✅ DONE | **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` **ALL GREEN** — invariants, lint (no new warnings), iOS **622 tests / 58 suites** `** TEST SUCCEEDED **`, macOS Debug build `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, whitespace clean. **Runtime-verified live** on real seeded Library data: confirmed the T-134 stat-escape fix still holds (no regression) at accessibility-XXXL; found, at default text size, a natural 3-card comparison in the "Downloaded" carousel — two works with only an "Offline" badge as their last element, and a third ("It only takes a few words," different rating/chapter-count/word-count metadata) with an active "Reading 3%" progress bar — the progress bar sits flush at the card's bottom with the same visual margin the "Offline"-only cards show below their own last element, confirming the pin holds consistently across cards with genuinely different metadata amounts, not just a single card compared against itself. |
| T-137 | **Scaled cards no longer exceed the screen width (owner: "individual cards should be no larger than screen width with some padding on the edges")** — T-136's `@ScaledMetric`-driven `ScaledCarouselCardSize` had no ceiling: at accessibility-XXXL the proportionally scaled width could exceed the device's actual screen width, clipping the card's trailing content off-screen (owner-confirmed via screenshot: "Harry Potter - J. K." and "2.3K"/badges cut off at the right edge). **Fix:** `ScaledCarouselCardSize` now computes an unclamped `scaledWidth`/`scaledHeight` pair (same as before) plus a `maxWidth` (screen/window width minus 32pt — this app's established 16pt-per-edge carousel/grid margin, see `WorkCarouselSection`/`LibraryEntityGridView`) and a `clampRatio = min(1, maxWidth / scaledWidth)` applied to **both** `width` and `height` together — so the 164:228 aspect ratio survives the clamp too (T-136's whole point), rather than only being preserved in the unclamped case. `maxWidth` reads `UIScreen.main.bounds.width` (iOS) / `NSScreen.main?.frame.width` (macOS), each behind `#if os(...)`. | Claude | hig-review | ✅ DONE | **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` **ALL GREEN** — invariants, lint, iOS **622 tests / 58 suites**, macOS Debug build (exercises the `NSScreen` branch), whitespace clean. **Runtime-verified live**: the same real saved work's card, previously clipped off the right edge of the screen at accessibility-XXXL (owner's own screenshot), now renders fully within the screen bounds with visible margin on both edges after the fix. |
| T-136 | **Scaled carousel cards grow both wider and taller, preserving aspect ratio (owner: "scaled cards should scale both ways to keep their aspect ratio")** — T-131/T-134 made `WorkSummaryCardSurface`/`ReadingQueueCard`/`CollectionCard` grow *taller* at large Dynamic Type sizes but left width pinned to the static 164pt `CarouselCardMetrics.width`, distorting the card's 164:228 aspect ratio into an increasingly tall, narrow shape. **Fix:** added `ScaledCarouselCardSize` (`UIComponents/CarouselCardStyle.swift`), a small `DynamicProperty`-conforming struct with two `@ScaledMetric(relativeTo: .body)` properties (`width`/`height`, base 164/228) — since both use the identical scale curve, their ratio stays exactly 164:228 at every Dynamic Type size. Embedded a `var cardSize = ScaledCarouselCardSize()` in all 5 carousel card views (`WorkSummaryCardSurface`, `ReadingQueueCard`, `NewReadingQueueCard`, `CollectionCard`, `NewCollectionCard`) and swapped their `CarouselCardMetrics.width`/`.height` references for `cardSize.width`/`.height` — the content-width pin from T-134 now uses the scaled width too, so `FlowLayout`'s ideal-height measurement stays consistent with the new (larger) rendered width. `LibraryEntityGridView`'s `.adaptive(minimum:)` grid also now feeds `cardSize.width` into `CarouselCardMetrics.adaptiveCardColumns(minimum:)` instead of the static constant, so its column-width assumption stays consistent with the now-wider cards (otherwise a scaled-wide card could overflow a column the grid sized against the unscaled 164pt minimum). `SkeletonLoading.swift`'s loading-state skeleton deliberately left unscaled in this pass — its internal placeholder blocks are hardcoded pixel sizes with no relation to `CarouselCardMetrics`, so scaling only the outer frame would look worse (tiny fixed bars floating in a much bigger card), not better; captured as a proposed **Wave 11** in `docs/audits/Fixes_and_HIG_Implementation_Plan.md` for a dedicated pass scaling the outer frame *and* every internal block together. **Compiler hazard hit and fixed along the way:** adding a defaulted `cardSize` parameter to `ReadingQueueCard`/`CollectionCard`/their `New*` siblings pushed `LibraryView.swift`'s already-long `.navigationDestination` modifier chain over Swift's type-checker complexity limit — a hard "unable to type-check this expression in reasonable time" build failure, not just a slow build. Added explicit non-defaulted `init()`s to all 4 card types (removing the ambiguous defaulted-parameter search branch) and extracted the two `LibraryEntityGridView(...)` call sites out of their inline `.navigationDestination` trailing closures into separately-typed `collectionsGridDestination`/`readingQueuesGridDestination` computed properties (an explicit `some View` return type boundary breaks the compiler's combined-expression search space) — both were needed together to actually resolve it. Also caught and fixed a second, unrelated bug of my own: `LibraryEntityGridView`'s new `cardSize` was initially declared `private`, which — per a real Swift gotcha — forces the *whole* compiler-synthesized memberwise init down to `private` too, breaking every other (needed, caller-supplied) parameter; removed the modifier. | Claude | hig-review | ✅ DONE | **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` **ALL GREEN** — invariants, lint (only pre-existing warnings, line numbers shifted), iOS **622 tests / 58 suites** `** TEST SUCCEEDED **`, macOS Debug build `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, whitespace clean. **Runtime-verified this session**, two sizes: at accessibility-XXXL, the same real saved work's card visibly widened (title now wraps at 2 full-width lines instead of truncating, "NR"+"1/1" and "Complete"+"2.3K" now pack two-per-row where they previously stacked one-per-row, confirming `FlowLayout` is genuinely using the extra width) with a comfortable — not cramped, not overflowing — margin from the proportional height floor; at the largest standard (non-accessibility) size, the whole card fit on one screen with all 6 metadata items visible and no overlap. **Not runtime-verified:** `LibraryEntityGridView`'s multi-column adaptive-grid layout specifically (this session's test library had no populated Collection/Reading Queue to populate a "See All" grid with 2+ real cards) — the `cardSize.width`-as-minimum change is a straightforward parameter substitution into an already-correct, well-established SwiftUI API (`.adaptive(minimum:)`), reasoned rather than visually confirmed. |

T-127's F1 refactor made `WorkReorderDropDelegate.dropEntered` assign `pendingOrder = ReadingQueueService.reorderedIDs(...)` *unconditionally*; `reorderedIDs` returns `base` unchanged for a self-hover (drag hovering its own source card — which fires at drag start, confirmed by T-126's own automation logs showing only `target == draggedWorkID`) or a cross-and-return, so `pendingOrder` flips from `nil` to a value **equal to the stored order**. The pre-T-127 code returned early on self-hover, leaving `pendingOrder` `nil`. Net regression: a drag that never actually reordered anything (pick up a card, release without crossing another) now reached `performDrop`'s `if let pendingOrder { ReadingQueueService.reorder(...) }`, which rewrites every membership's `sortOrderInQueue`, calls `markModified()` on **all** of them (→ `.pending` sync state), and `saveBestEffort` — a disk write plus whole-queue sync churn for a no-op. No corruption/crash; pure write amplification. **Fix (one condition, `ReadingQueues.swift` `performDrop`):** commit only when `pendingOrder != works.map(\.id)` — `works` is unmutated during the gesture (the point of deferring the write), so it's still the pre-drag order; this catches both the self-hover and cross-and-return no-op paths, where a `dropEntered`-side guard would miss the latter. Not unit-coverable (view-layer drag-lifecycle state transition, not the pure `reorderedIDs` function T-127 already tests); reasoned + code-correct, and folds into the same real-device drag-commit spot-check T-126/T-127 already carry forward. | Claude | `claude/app-version-commit-sha-427004` | ✅ DONE | **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` **ALL GREEN** — invariants, lint, iOS **618 tests / 57 suites** (unchanged; the guard is view-layer), macOS Debug build, whitespace. **Carried forward:** the compact-grid drag-*commit* real-device/manual-trackpad spot-check (unchanged from T-126/T-127) now also implicitly covers "a no-op drag persists nothing." |
