.isSelected : [])`, matching the sibling `filterChip` (`LibraryView.swift:444`) that already had the trait — without it VoiceOver announced the row but never said whether it was currently selected. **UI-2 — `RecentlyDeletedRow`** was 3 disconnected VoiceOver stops (title/subtitle/days-left) with zero a11y modifiers; now conditionally combines into one via `.accessibilityElement(children: .combine)` **only** when `authorIdentities.isEmpty` (a plain, non-interactive subtitle `Text`) — when identities are present, the subtitle renders via `AO3AuthorBylineView`, whose author names can be individually VoiceOver-focusable/navigable (`AO3AuthorNavigation`), and blanket-combining would have swept those into one non-interactive element and silently removed that navigation from a Recently Deleted row. **UI-2 — `WorkCardActions.swift` investigated, no change needed:** its cited "17 controls, 0 a11y modifiers" is a systemic-sweep grep count, but every one of those controls is a `Button`+`Label` inside a native `.contextMenu`, not a custom Button-wrapped row like Wave 3's `cyclingFacetRow` — native context-menu items get solid built-in VoiceOver support (the icon-leak/no-combine failure mode Wave 1-3 kept fixing is specific to custom-styled rows, not `.contextMenu` content). **UI-7 — `LibraryView`'s dashboard bulk-select ported to macOS.** The whole feature (`selectList`, the "Select" toolbar entry, `enterSelectMode`/`selectAction`/`exitSelectMode`, `localCarouselCard`'s selecting branch) was `#if os(iOS)`-only, gated on a **local `EditMode` that was never actually bound to any List's `\.editMode` environment** — pure vestigial state with no real iOS-only requirement, unlike `LibrarySectionListView`'s sibling implementation one level down, which already used a plain cross-platform `@State private var isSelecting: Bool`. Replaced `LibraryView`'s `EditMode` with the same plain-Bool pattern and removed every platform gate around the feature; the toolbar's existing `WorkBulkActionBar` macOS placement (`.primaryAction` vs. iOS's `.bottomBar`) was already correctly per-platform and untouched. **Runtime-verified end-to-end on native macOS `Kudos.app`** (computer-use): the "..." menu's "Select" entry is now present, tapping it enters select mode (title → "Select Works", every card gets a selection circle), tapping a card shows "1 Selected" with an accent-blue border + checkmark, and the toolbar's Select All/privacy/list-view/trash/Actions controls all respond — exited cleanly via the checkmark. Tab-bar-hide (P3, iOS-only chrome, no macOS equivalent) and dual-bulk-select-implementation's "non-modal" hiding critique were left untouched — the code's own existing comment already documents this as a deliberate tradeoff for a real Liquid Glass color-bleed artifact, and the audit's own recommendation is "fix the bleed, not the chrome," which is out of scope here. **A6-F1 — Reading Queue compact-grid drag-to-reorder, owner-confirmed broken despite surviving T-77's full adversarial review, root-caused and fixed.** Reproduced live: `WorkReorderDropDelegate.dropEntered` called `ReadingQueueService.reorder(_:)` directly on every drag-over crossing, mutating `queue.memberships` — a SwiftData relationship `ReadingQueueDetailView` observes — which re-rendered the compact grid's `ForEach` and rebuilt every card's drop delegate **while the OS drag session driving that same delegate was still active**, interrupting it; the drag would visibly start (source card dims) but never complete, and nothing was ever persisted. Fixed by deferring the SwiftData write: new `@State private var pendingCompactOrder: [UUID]?` holds the live drag preview (read by a new `compactDisplayedWorks` computed property feeding the grid's `ForEach`), `dropEntered` now only updates `pendingCompactOrder`, and `ReadingQueueService.reorder(_:)` is called exactly once, in `performDrop`, after the gesture actually ends — keeping the drag session's view identities stable for its whole duration. The detailed list's native `List`+`.onMove` reorder was investigated and left untouched: unlike the compact grid's custom per-drag-over `DropDelegate` callback, `.onMove` only fires once when a gesture completes, so it never had this failure mode. **Verification is honest and partial, not full end-to-end live reproduction of the fix:** the wiring — `onDrag` correctly setting `draggedWorkID`, `dropEntered`'s self-drop guard (`draggedWorkID != target.id`) correctly no-op'ing without mutating anything, `performDrop`'s cleanup — was live-verified via temporary `Log.library.fault(...)` instrumentation (OSLog; plain `print()` doesn't reach `log stream`) added, tested, and fully removed before commit. But the actual cross-card commit path (`dropEntered` firing with a *different* card as target, then `performDrop` committing `pendingOrder`) could **not** be triggered via synthetic mouse automation in the simulator despite 6 varied attempts (large jumps, long holds, the `left_click_drag` primitive, and a 12-step smooth trajectory) — every attempt's log showed only the self-drop no-op case, `target == draggedWorkID`, never a foreign target. This is consistent with a genuine automation-tooling limitation specific to `NSItemProvider`-based `UIDragInteraction` (as opposed to `List`'s simpler built-in cell-reorder gesture): the **detailed list's native reorder was, by contrast, successfully live-dragged and confirmed to persist** across the same session using the identical mouse-drag technique, proving the technique itself works when the underlying gesture recognizer is the simpler one. **Recommend the owner spot-check the compact-grid drag on a real device (or via direct trackpad interaction, not automation) before considering A6-F1 fully closed** — the fix is reasoned-correct and its non-drag wiring is proven, but its core commit path is unexercised by this session's live testing. Sibling `items` confirmed safe by construction (guards `totalPages > 1`; its `(currentPage-1)...(currentPage+1)` window range is always valid; the `where` clause filters into bounds — out-of-contract state degrades to `[1][…][last]` with no highlighted pill instead of trapping) — left unchanged. 2 new tests / 8 assertions in `KudosTests/SearchPaginationTests.swift` pin the normal windows (mid/first/last/single-page) and the formerly-trapping inputs ((7,5)→4...5, (6,5)→4...5, (0,5)→1...2, (1,0)→1...1). | T-122 | **App version shows the build's commit SHA** — Settings → About (and bug reports, which reuse `AboutView.versionString` in `BugReportView`) now display `1.0 (1) · <short-sha>`. Implementation: the `project.pbxproj` script phase formerly named "Add Background Modes to Info.plist (iOS only)" was widened into **"Inject Info.plist keys (git SHA + iOS background modes)"** — it stamps a `GitCommitSHA` key (`git -C "$SRCROOT" rev-parse --short=7 HEAD`) into the processed Info.plist on **all platforms** (background-modes injection stays iOS-only inside a `case "${PLATFORM_NAME}"` guard; it also now writes via `${TARGET_BUILD_DIR}/${INFOPLIST_PATH}` instead of `${CODESIGNING_FOLDER_PATH}/Info.plist`, which was iOS-only-correct). When git or the repo is unavailable (e.g. source-tarball build) the script warns and skips, the key is simply absent, and `versionString` falls back to the old `1.0 (1)` format. A second Info.plist-writing phase was not an option — two phases can't both declare the processed plist as an output. **⚠️ Required flipping `ENABLE_USER_SCRIPT_SANDBOXING` YES→NO (both project-level configs) — owner veto welcome:** the sandbox whitelists only *literal* declared input paths, but git reads branch-dependent loose refs + pack files under `.git/` that can't be enumerated statically. Proven empirically by replaying Xcode's generated `.sb` profile via `sandbox-exec` against a plain clone: `git rev-parse HEAD` dies with `fatal: Needed a single revision`; it only worked in this git *worktree* by accident (a worktree's real gitdir lives outside `SRCROOT`, i.e. outside the sandbox's `deny file-read*` subpath rules — so worktree-only testing would have masked the breakage in normal checkouts). All script phases here are first-party, so the lost guardrail is minimal; if unacceptable, the alternative is an unsandboxed helper aggregate target that writes the SHA to a derived file the (re-sandboxed) main phase declares as input. | Claude | `claude/app-version-commit-sha-427004` | ✅ DONE | **Verified:** iPhone 17 (iOS 26.5) sim build injects `GitCommitSHA=a021573` and PlistBuddy confirms it in the built `Kudos.app/Info.plist`; build log shows zero `sandbox-exec` wrappers post-flip; macOS build log shows the same injection line. `Scripts/verify.sh` **ALL GREEN** — invariants, lint, iOS **610 tests / 57 suites**, macOS Debug build, whitespace. Not runtime-screenshot-verified in the About sheet UI (text-only change to an existing `Text` line). |

the file's own comment now explicitly warns against reintroducing one. **CardNavigationModifier** (`UIComponents/AppThemeSurface.swift`, owner: "don't defer, fix it right") — `cardNavigation(to:)` gained a required `accessibilityLabel: String` parameter, applied to the invisible background `NavigationLink` along with `.accessibilityAddTraits(.isButton)` and `.accessibilityHidden` gated on the existing `cardNavigationSuppressed` flag, threaded through all 16 call sites (each passing the row's own title) — deliberately NOT combining/hiding the row's other elements, so nested fandom-search/expand/byline controls stay independently VoiceOver-reachable; this was chosen over the fuller "one combined stop per card" rewrite specifically to avoid that trade-off. **WorkCardListControls** — no code change; owner confirmed leaving the "Clear All Filters" destructive styling as-is after research found it already matches 3 established sibling conventions (`AO3FilterPanel`/`LibraryFilterPanel`'s "Reset Filters", `CustomizeThemeView`'s "Reset Theme" — all `role: .destructive`, zero confirmation, for recoverable actions); A6-F2's "bulk-action bar lives here" plan note confirmed to mean the `UIComponents/` directory in general, not this file specifically — no bulk-action-bar code exists in `WorkCardListControls.swift`. **Adversarial review caught and fixed a real bug before it shipped:** an independent multi-lens review of the CardNavigationModifier change found that 4 call sites (`AccountComponents.swift`, `AO3AccountWorksList.swift`, `AO3SeriesDetailView.swift`, `AuthorProfileContentSections.swift`) wrapped a `SensitiveWorkRow` in an *external* `.cardNavigation(...)` even though `SensitiveWorkRow` already applies it *internally* (`Features/Privacy/MatureContent.swift`) for its non-blurred branch — a pre-existing double-wrap that was harmless while unlabeled, but became a **privacy bypass** once this change made the label mandatory and real: for a blurred Mature/Explicit work, the outer link sat behind the "Tap to reveal" gate but was not itself hidden or gated, so VoiceOver could reach it directly, read the real (supposedly hidden) title, and navigate straight to the work — skipping `PrivacyGate`'s reveal step entirely. Fixed by removing the redundant external wrap at all 4 sites; independently re-verified after the fix. Two further findings from the same review were confirmed real but left as-is (both pre-existing, both minor): `AO3SeriesSummary.title`/`AO3Collection.title` parsing (`Services/AO3Client+Authors.swift:196`, `AO3Client.swift:406`) has no empty-string guard, so a blank AO3-side title would theoretically produce an empty accessibility label — same gap the row's own visible `Text` already has, not introduced by this wave, deferred as a `Services/` parsing concern outside this wave's scope. | Claude | `claude/hig-wave2-uicomponents-775ed22` | ✅ DONE | **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN — invariants, lint (only pre-existing warnings, incl. `ReadingQueues.swift`'s file-length warning which predates this wave at 847 lines), iOS **602 tests / 57 suites** (iPhone 17/iOS 26.5), macOS Debug build, whitespace. One verify run mid-session showed a failure (`ReadiumProgressPersistenceTests.noiseDoesNotArmATrailingWrite`) caused by my own concurrent `simctl terminate`/`launch` calls killing the test runner's app process mid-suite ("Restarting after unexpected exit, crash, or test timeout") — confirmed as self-inflicted, not a regression, by a clean re-run immediately after with zero other changes. **Remains manual (owner):** the AO3AuthorNavigation hit-target fix's `.top`-alignment reasoning is sound by SwiftUI's frame/baseline semantics but genuinely unverified at runtime — this file has a documented history of an almost-identical technique failing visually in exactly this spot, so a live check (a multi-coauthor byline, both `compact` and full-width) is warranted before Wave 3 reuses the pattern; same for WorkCarouselSection's ~15pt header-height growth and TagChip's `FlowLayout` reflow at 28pt. | T-113 | **Search typing hang + index completeness** — typing in Global Search hung seconds per keystroke. Root cause: `FandomCatalog.cachedFandoms` scanned the whole cached AO3 fandom catalog per keystroke with `.lowercased()` allocations inside the sort comparator, ×2 evaluations per render, no debounce (the library side already used `WorkSearchIndex` correctly). Fix: precomputed normalized+deduped+count-sorted catalog search index (rebuilt lazily on catalog change, `revision`-stamped for observers) with an allocation-free prefix-first ranked scan that early-exits at the limit; SearchView's five per-render match lists replaced by one debounced (150 ms) `.task(id:)` snapshot keyed on query + record counts + catalog revision, with `modelContext != nil` liveness guards on snapshot models; `WorkSearchIndex` v2 adds `seriesTitle` + user-tag names — reindex added at WorkDetailView tag add/remove, KudosBackup restore reindex moved *after* user-tag linking, launch sweep migrates v1 records; catalog + local name matching unified on diacritic-folded `WorkSearchIndex.normalize` (typing "pokemon" now matches "Pokémon"; old code couldn't); `WorkRow` strips summary HTML 1× per render instead of 3×. Audited every `SavedWork` mutation path for reindex coverage — importer ×5, WorkTags ×2, metadata refresh, queue service, backup restore all covered; folder-sync/canonical-merge mutate no indexed fields. | Claude | `claude/search-indexing-performance-3d5408` | ✅ DONE | Committed `cf79177` (2026-07-17). **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN (invariants, lint, full iOS suite on iPhone 17/iOS 26.5, macOS Debug build, whitespace); targeted suites 8 `WorkSearchIndexTests` + 4 new `FandomCatalogSearchTests` all pass. **Simulator (seen, iPhone 17 + real warm catalog & library):** per-keystroke typing updates live with no hang; "harry" → Fandoms on AO3 ranked Harry Potter (599K) first then count-desc; substring matches work ("ahrr" → "Fahrrad fahr'n"); library works section matches; clear → instant idle; Return still runs the real AO3 search (4.7k pages rendered). **Remains manual:** owner feel-check typing latency on the physical device with their full library. **Follow-up (same day, owner reported still hanging on device):** three more per-keystroke scan sites found + the real device-side culprit — the one-time v2 launch migration reindexed the whole library in ~200-record main-actor bursts (measured ~1ms/record on M-series in-memory; multiples of that on-device), freezing typing for its whole duration. Fixed: (1) `rebuildIfNeeded` now time-sliced (≤5 ms main-actor slices + 2 ms breathers) with incremental saves every 250 records (bounds autosave churn; a force-quit mid-sweep resumes instead of restarting); (2) `FandomListView` (Browse → category filter, up to ~20k names) — precomputed normalized entries + 120 ms debounced snapshot instead of per-keystroke `localizedCaseInsensitiveContains` + full-list re-diff; (3) `LibraryOptionPicker` (filter-panel tag lists) — options normalized once per presentation, filter evaluated once per render; (4) `AddWorksToCollectionView` "Filter works" — now matches via `WorkSearchIndex` (index text, AND-across-terms; richer + no per-keystroke lowercase/fandom-decode rescans), candidates/matches evaluated once per render. New scale test: 1,200-work sweep completes exactly once across save/slice boundaries. **⚠️ Install-provenance correction (2026-07-17 evening):** the two earlier device installs shipped a **stale Jul 15 `.app` from a different DerivedData folder** (picked via `ls …/Debug-iphoneos/Kudos.app \| head -1` instead of the invoking build's own products dir) — the phone never ran the first fix, so the owner's "still hanging" report was made against pre-T-106/pre-T-113 code (the follow-up's migration-slicing + extra filter-surface fixes remain real hazards and stand on their own). Verified-current build (strings-checked: no stale `"Shortcuts & Preferences"`, new symbols present; product of this worktree's DerivedData, Jul 17 18:08) installed on the owner's iPhone + re-verified in the simulator on the same product: search sections render instantly and T-106's Overview-card removal is visible (owner's "extra Overview button" report was the stale binary — no code change needed, `07cb369a` already removed it). **Lesson: always take the `.app` path from the build log's own products dir and strings-check provenance before installing.** **Merged to `release-fixes`** (fast-forward `0fbe2e6..8e5fadc`, 2026-07-17, pushed) — this brought T-106–T-112 (`claude/general-improvements-branch-87e11b`) along, since T-113 was stacked on that branch's tip. Task branches/worktrees for the merged stack cleaned up on owner instruction; `release-fixes` remains the integration tip. |

Covers: `AO3Client` pagination-total loop (4 sites → shared `paginationTotal(in:currentPage:)`, widened to `Element` so the Comments call site can scope it to `#comments_placeholder`) and `stat`/`statInt` digit-parsing (unified on the more-robust `.filter(\.isNumber)` strategy, fixing the inconsistency the audit flagged as the actual defect); the two authenticated-write-action items done with the extra care §9 called for — CSRF-fetch (`AO3WriteActions`/`AO3CommentActions`, 8 call sites → one `fetchCSRFPage(at:)` returning `(html, token)`; `giveKudos`'s 2xx/422 kudos-specific switch deliberately left alone, per the audit's own instruction); `WorkLifecycle.saveBestEffort` ×5 sites → new `ModelContext.saveBestEffort(reason:)` in `Utilities/`; Account external-nav URL builder → new `AccountExternalNavCard` in `AccountComponents.swift`; preference-key stripping ×4 → new `preferenceFieldKey(_:)` in `AO3PreferencesModels.swift`; `FandomCatalog.loadMissing`/`refresh` → shared private `fetch(_:onlyStale:)`; series-progress-text → shared `SeriesPreservationResult.summaryParts(verb:)` (kept the phase-specific cancelled/empty/prefix copy separate per surface — the two functions have genuinely different progress-vs-completion architectures, not just cosmetic differences, so a full merge would have been the wrong call); §4.6 turned out to be **two** distinct duplicate pairs, not one shared component as the audit's text implied — `HomeCards`' cover-card stats (rating-short/chapters/completion/word-count, compact notation) and `WorkRow`/`AO3WorkRow`'s list-row stats (rating-full/word-count-full/chapters/**kudos**, not completion) are different field sets/formatting/spacing, so built `CoverCardStatsRow` and `WorkListStatsRow` separately rather than forcing one shape; `AccountComponents.swift` was already over its 700-line soft threshold before this session and grew further, still just a warning not a hard error), iOS **552 tests / 52 suites** (same count — structural only, no tests added/lost), macOS Debug build, whitespace. **Session C committed** `252c433` (§4.3c) + `05fec6b` (§4.1) + `441d103` (§4.5) — the needs-behavioral-verification tranche, one commit per finding (this tier is the one worth reverting piecemeal). §4.3c: `AO3AuthorProfileModel`'s three authenticated actions (Subscribe, block/mute begin, block/mute confirm) now share one `performFencedAuthorAction` with the stale-response fencing preserved exactly — success path re-checks route+scope+generation, both failure paths re-check scope+generation only (matching the originals: an error's side effects are session-wide, not route-scoped), and expiry is reported against the *captured* generation so a stale response can never log out a replacement session; the user-EPUB matcher gains the canonical-URL tier it was missing; the audit's §9 open question on `existingWork(forSource:)`'s exact-`sourceURL` 4th tier answered **yes, keep** — it's the only identity signal for non-AO3/non-canonicalizable sources — retained as a documented final tier after the index lookup; the title/author/file-size heuristic for sourceless user EPUBs stays (similarity, not identity, so it doesn't belong in the index); 2 new `PreservedWorkTests` cover the §9-required dedup paths (download-URL-variant merge via the canonical tier; exact non-AO3 merge). Tier-priority nuance recorded in the commit message: strongest-tier-first can pick a different record than the old fetch-order OR-scan only when the library *already* holds same-work duplicates (the degenerate state `CanonicalWorkMerge` exists to repair). Also dropped `WorkImporter`'s now-superfluous `file_length` lint disable. §4.5: the 4× duplicated selection/bulk-action shell (`FandomWorksView`, `TagWorksView`, `SearchView`, `AuthorProfileView`) collapsed into new `UIComponents/RemoteWorkSelection.swift` — `RemoteWorkSelectionController` (@Observable: the nine formerly-per-view @State properties + toggle/exit/selected(in:) + the four bulk ops), `RemoteWorkSelectionToolbar`, `RemoteWorkBulkActionBar`, `SelectableAO3WorkRow` (the byte-identical row wrapper 3 of the 4 surfaces shared), and `.remoteWorkSelectionChrome()` (sheets + failure alert + cancel-on-disappear); net −280 lines (631 deleted across the views). Key design decision: chrome + batch task stay **host-side**, not bar-side — bar-owned state would cancel an in-flight batch and drop its failure alert/Add-to sheet the moment Done swaps the toolbar branch; host ownership preserves pre-consolidation behavior exactly. Rows/data sources, pagination, overlays, and non-selecting toolbars stay per-surface (genuinely different, per the audit's own "parameterized shell, not monolithic view" guidance); bulk-button enablement still keys off `selection.isEmpty`, so stale-selection-after-refresh behaves as before. **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN three times (after §4.3c: iOS **559 tests / 53 suites**; at the Session C tip twice: **561 / 53** — +7 fence tests, +2 import tests), macOS Debug build each time; regression-matrix rows updated (author profiles, multi-select shell, EPUB import). **Owner still owed (audit §9):** manual selection/bulk/pagination pass on all 4 surfaces + a live-session Subscribe/Block/Mute spot-check — structural-only changes, but they sit on the bulk-action and authenticated-write surfaces. **Session D explicitly skipped by owner decision** (generic `TTLCache` — too much risk for too little codebase-size/efficiency gain). **Session E (research pass on the audit's 6 `investigate` leads, §6) done** — no source changes, a research report only, committed as a `## 10` addendum to `docs/audits/PONYTAIL_AUDIT_RELEASE_FIXES.md`. Ran as a `Workflow` (6 finder agents, one per lead, each verdict adversarially verified by a second agent — same methodology as the original audit's Pass 2); one verifier call died mid-response on a transient API error and was recovered by resuming the same run from cache (11 of 12 agent calls replayed instantly, only the failed one re-ran). 2 of 6 initial verdicts needed a real correction under verification, not just confirmation: **§6.1** (4+ duplicate AO3-href-to-absolute-URL resolvers, turned out to be 10 across 6 named functions + 4 inline) — the duplication is real, but the finder's "credential-leak risk" framing was refuted: every write path funnels through `AO3AuthService.authenticatedRequest`'s `isTrustedURL` gate regardless, so this is a correctness/robustness cleanup, not an open vulnerability; **§6.6** (`WorkDetailView.withLocalWork` vs `ReadingQueueService.resolveLocalWork`) — the consolidation itself is still confirmed safe, but the finder's specific safety argument (leaning on `PreservedWorkService.softDelete`'s docstring) was wrong — `ReadingQueueService.normalize(_:)` can leave a soft-deleted work's EPUB missing on disk, contradicting the docstring's premise — the verifier traced the *actual* safety net (the `hasReadableEPUB`/`restoreReadableEPUB` self-heal chain at read-time, plus `preserve()`'s own recheck at queue-add) that makes the consolidation safe for a different reason than originally claimed. The other 4 verdicts held under verification: **§6.2** (`SyncMerge.deterministicMembershipOrder`) confirmed dead, safe to delete, as mechanical as any §3 finding; **§6.3** (`FolderSyncService.foldConflictContents`) confirmed *not* a simple duplicate — found a real bug along the way (production's `foldFileProviderConflicts` silently discards restore summaries, so the sync-result counts shown in Settings undercount whenever a File-Provider conflict is folded), needs an owner product decision, not a delete; **§6.4** (`AccountWorksInlineSection` vs `AO3AccountWorksList`) and **§6.5** (`AccountView`'s two root layouts) both confirmed genuinely distinct / mostly false positives — retain, close both leads. Full per-lead evidence, citations, and recommended next actions in the audit doc's new §10. **Session E follow-through implemented and committed** (`cc7ae02`/`db52216`/`099a727`, one commit per finding matching the Session C convention): (1) §6.2 — deleted `SyncMerge.deterministicMembershipOrder` + its sole test, zero behavior change. (2) §6.3 — owner decided to fix the undercount: `foldFileProviderConflicts` now returns a `FolderSyncResult` (was a bare `Int`) via the same `absorb`-per-version pattern `foldConflictContents` already used, keeping the exact per-version throw/resolution semantics; new `FolderSyncResult.absorb(_ other: FolderSyncResult)` overload lets `performSyncDown` fold it into its own total instead of only keeping the count; new unit test on the overload itself (the NSFileVersion conflict path stays untestable in isolation, unchanged limitation). (3) §6.6 — `WorkDetailView.withLocalWork` now calls `ReadingQueueService.resolveLocalWork` directly (mirroring `WorkCardActions.performRemoteAction`'s existing wrapper pattern), deleting the duplicated download/chapter-count/import/apply-metadata sequence and the now-unused `applyRemoteMetadata` wrapper; the diff cites the *corrected* safety argument from §10.6 (the `hasReadableEPUB`/`restoreReadableEPUB` self-heal chain at `read()` + `preserve()`'s own recheck at queue-add), not the disproven `softDelete` docstring premise; new regression test locks in that `resolveLocalWork`'s fast path revives a soft-deleted work with a genuinely-missing EPUB without silently marking it readable. §6.1 (href resolvers) and §6.4/§6.5 (retained leads) were left untouched, per scope — §6.1 was flagged as a future correctness cleanup, not urgent, and wasn't part of what was greenlit this round. **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN — iOS **562 tests / 53 suites** (net +1: two new regression tests, one deleted dead-code test), macOS Debug build, whitespace, lint clean in every touched file. **Merged into `release-fixes` (`0f68363`, 2026-07-16) on owner instruction after the live-test fixes landed** — the merged tree is byte-identical to the verify.sh-ALL-GREEN branch tip. **Live-test fixes (2026-07-16):** the owner's simulator + physical-iPhone pass found 5 issues; all resolved (one commit per fix), continuing the interrupted first fix session's uncommitted work after re-verifying every change against the code. (1) Comment "Edit" menu item missing → **expected AO3 behavior, not an app bug** (`320e690`): otwarchive's `comments_helper.rb#can_edit_comment?` renders Edit only on the session's *own* comment with *zero replies* and not frozen, while Delete additionally appears on any comment on the user's own works — a replied-to comment showing Delete-but-no-Edit is AO3's rule, and the app's contract is to expose exactly what AO3 rendered; parser confirmed correct against live markup, fixture action-list realigned to the live capture (`data-remote` links, `delete_comment?id=` href shape, Freeze-Thread `button_to` the anchor-only parser skips). (2) Work-level Subscribe/Bookmark labels never reflected state (`f6f01f2`): new advisory `AO3AuthService.workActionStates` work-page read (fetched when menu content appears, signed-in only, once per work + refetched after each relevant write) + new `AO3Client.parseExistingBookmark` (AO3 embeds the user's bookmark as an *edit* form — `/bookmarks/<id>` + `_method=put`, prefilled — vs. a create form when unbookmarked; both captured live into fixtures) drive conditional "Unsubscribe"/"Edit Bookmark on AO3" labels; the composer prefills the existing bookmark and `saveBookmark` (was `createBookmark`) routes create-vs-update from the *fresh* work page at submit time so a stale label can never duplicate-create, carrying collection names through untouched; +2 tests. (3) "Mark for Later" always failed (`588a712`): AO3's control is a `button_to` carrying `_method=patch` (captured live) — the body was missing the Rails method override so the POST matched no route; pre-existing bug (Session B's CSRF-helper rename was byte-identical), never live-verified before the owner's pass. (4) Block/Mute confirm silently no-op'd (`99fbb50`): SwiftUI writes the alert's `isPresented = false` *before* running the tapped button's action, and the binding's dismiss handler called `cancelPendingModeration()` — clearing the very submit snapshot that exists to survive dismiss — so Confirm's Task found nothing to submit; new dismiss-only `moderationAlertDidDismiss()` preserves the snapshot (explicit Cancel still drops both), +2 fence tests locking in dismiss-vs-cancel; the rest of the chain audited clean — `submitScrapedHTMLForm` submits the scraped fields (incl. `_method`) wholesale over the same `writeRequest`/`submitWrite` path the live-confirmed author-Subscribe uses, and `parseAuthorModerationForm` scrapes every `input[name]` of the matched blocked/muted form (the alert rendering correct labels live proves the parse), so the cleared snapshot was the whole bug. (5) Recently Deleted restore invisible (`d244374`): restore existed only as a leading swipe — undiscoverable on iOS, and no mouse path at all on macOS — rows now also expose Restore/Delete-Permanently in a context menu (long-press iOS, right-click macOS), same handlers. Regression-matrix rows updated (`aaa935c`). **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN at the tip — iOS **566 tests / 53 suites** (+4: 2 dismiss-vs-cancel fence, 2 work-page-state), macOS Debug build, lint pre-existing-only, whitespace; the fixed build is installed on the iPhone 17 simulator ready to test. **Next: owner live-tests the 5 fixes (esp. the never-live-verified writes: mark-for-later POST, bookmark save/update + label states, block/mute confirm submit) + runs the §9 manual checks (live-session Subscribe/Block/Mute + selection/bulk/pagination on the 4 remote-work surfaces), decides on §6.1 (correctness cleanup, not urgent) if/when wanted, and reviews everything (merge to `release-fixes` already made at the owner's instruction; release-fixes-to-main stays a separate later call).** |
| T-103 | **Comments audit fixes Part C — account/generation isolation (CAA-3, CAA-11, CAA-16).** Scope Comments page/chapter caches, async loading/submission/verification, and drafts to immutable authentication identity + session generation; prevent stale continuations and anonymous fallback. | Codex (finished by Claude after Codex ran out of usage mid-review-fix) | `codex/comments-account-isolation-caa3` (from `origin/release-fixes` `a3a3966`) | ✅ DONE | **CAA-3:** `CommentsModel` captures an immutable `AuthContext` (bare-username identity / cache scope / `sessionGeneration`) snapshot per load/submit/verification and re-checks it after every await (`isCurrent`); `CommentsPageCache.Key` **and** the chapter-index cache carry scope + generation, so a same-username relogin cold-loads and an owner's `/navigate` draft-chapter titles never serve across accounts or sign-out; required reads/verification surface auth-request construction failure as `AO3Error.authenticationRequired` (no `try?` anonymous downgrade — the last two sat in `verifyCommentPosted`, now throwing); `CommentsView` resets + force-reloads (or, for Inbox destinations, dismisses) on generation change via one `onChange(of: authenticationKey)`. **CAA-11:** draft keys are account-scoped (`identity|w-c-p`; unknown-username sessions stay `unknown-session:<generation>`-local; the "AO3 Account" placeholder never becomes an identity), the same bare-username identity now flows through adoption/submission/verification-clearing/logout consistently (Sol finding 5), and `postComment`/`postCommentReply`/`editComment`/`deleteComment` take a required `expectedGeneration` re-checked via `requireSessionGeneration` at entry **and** immediately after the form GET, so an account switch between form GET and POST aborts pre-POST (Sol finding 1); stale successful/failed submits and stale verifications are inert against the replacement account's composer/drafts/guard/page (findings 2, 8), `CommentSubmissionGuard.resetForAuthenticationChange()` unwinds busy presentation state without touching durable entries (finding 3), transition reset also clears `scope`/`selectedChapter`/`workContext` (findings 4, 9), and `CommentsView.delete` captures + fences its generation end-to-end. **CAA-16:** verification captures `pendingKey` (and identity) *before* resolution nils it, and `CommentDraftStore.clearVariants` clears every chapter sibling on verified or definitive success (finding 6). **Inbox-destination hole (Sol follow-up):** `AccountView` now passes `destination.sessionGeneration` → `CommentsView(requiredSessionGeneration:)`; every public `CommentsModel` entry is gated by `belongsToCurrentSession`, so a pre-first-load account change renders nothing and dismisses instead of loading Alice's private context under Bob. **Post-cutoff finish (Claude):** Codex's session died mid-final-re-review with 2 tests failing on the runner's second same-process suite execution (process-global `CommentsPageCache` static + test fakes restarting generation numbers → run-2 cache-key collisions); fixed by injecting the cache (`CommentsModel(pageCache:)`, production unchanged via `CommentsPageCache.shared`) with a fresh instance per test — the exact failing invocation (parallel-testing double execution) now passes 22/22; also moved `cachedCount` into `AccountView`'s existing private extension because the one-line `requiredSessionGeneration:` addition pushed the struct body to 901 lines (SwiftLint hard error at >900, caught by verify's lint gate after Codex's last green run). **Tests:** new `CommentsAccountTransitionTests` (11 deterministic cases: gated stale page/chapter completions inert, stale Inbox destination never loads, same-username relogin cold-loads, no anonymous fallback incl. verification throwing `authenticationRequired`, drafts per-account + restored on relogin, unknown-username drafts generation-local, stale submit/verification can't touch Bob's composer/guard/drafts, stale owner Edit/Reply/Delete refuse, chapter→work-scope verified success clears sibling drafts) + `CommentSubmissionTests` store-level variant-clearing case; reuses the Inbox suite's `Signal`/fakes (made internal). **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN — invariants, lint (pre-existing warnings only), iOS **552 tests / 52 suites** (iPhone 17 / iOS 26.5; **540 → 552** from the Part B baseline), macOS Debug build, whitespace; targeted double-execution run additionally green (22/22). | T-95 | **Inbox reply verification and durable duplicate-post guards** — close release-blocking findings T91-RF1 (ambiguous Inbox replies verified a guessed work-comments page instead of the exact parent thread) and T91-RF2 (the unresolved duplicate-post guard was model-local and lost across target changes/screen recreation) from `docs/RELEASE_READINESS_FABLE5.md`. | Claude | `release-fixes` (developed on `claude/inbox-duplicate-guards-470eba`, based on `claude/reader-completion-progress-fa8b77`) | ✅ DONE | **T91-RF1:** `AO3AuthService.verifyCommentPosted` (`Services/AO3CommentActions.swift`) now routes a reply's verification through a pure `verificationPlan(for:)` — a reply always resolves to `.standaloneThread(parentCommentID:)`, fetched via `AO3Client.commentThreadPage(commentID:)` (`/comments/<parentCommentID>`), never a work-comments page guess; top-level comments keep the existing page-1/last-page plan. The prior `knownPage` parameter (which fed a hardcoded `currentPageNumber = 1` from focused-thread loads straight into a work-comments page fetch — the exact false-negative-into-`.absent` double-post path the finding described) is gone. `containsComment` gained an optional `postedAfter` timing check (10-minute tolerance for clock skew/minute-granularity AO3 timestamps) so a coincidental old reply with the same author/parent/text can't be mistaken for proof THIS attempt landed; when a candidate's timestamp didn't parse, the check is skipped rather than false-negatived. **T91-RF2:** new `UnresolvedCommentSubmissionStore` (`Services/CommentSubmission.swift`) is a process-lifetime, auth-scoped (keyed by identity, then by full `CommentSubmissionKey`) record of ambiguous submissions that every `CommentSubmissionGuard` instance reads/writes through instead of holding a single local `pendingKey` slot. `CommentsModel.submissionGuard` shares `UnresolvedCommentSubmissionStore.shared`, so a guard recreated by target switching, popping to Inbox and reopening, or Comments-screen recreation rehydrates its blocked state from the store via the new `adopt(_:)` (called from `startComposer`, now `auth`-scoped) instead of resetting to idle. Distinct keys (different target, or genuinely different text) coexist independently — resolving one never touches another. `AO3AuthService.logout()`/`clearStoredSession()` each capture the outgoing session's username before clearing it and call `store.clear(identity:)`, so one account's unresolved state can't leak into or block whichever session follows. Entries are in-memory only (no cookies/CSRF/HTML/persisted-to-disk text) and expire after 1 hour if never resolved. **Tests (18 new):** `AO3CommentsParseTests` +7 (`verificationPlan` always resolves a reply to the standalone thread regardless of "page," a top-level context resolves to `.workComments`, a standalone-thread-shaped page containing the reply matches, timing tolerance accepts a plausible-clock-skew match / rejects a pre-existing unrelated reply / skips the check when `postedAt` didn't parse); `CommentSubmissionTests` +11 (A→B→A distinct-target isolation, ambiguous-A-pop-reopen, brand-new-guard model recreation, resolving one key leaves another blocked, cross-account key isolation, logout clears the logged-out account's entries, three concurrent unresolved keys never collide, store anchors `submittedAt` to the *original* attempt across a "Check Again" re-mark, `maxAge` eviction, `adopt` shows idle for an untracked key, `adopt` never clobbers an in-flight submission). **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN — invariants, lint (pre-existing warnings only), full iOS suite **446 tests / 47 suites** (iPhone 17 / iOS 26.5), macOS Debug build, whitespace; iOS + macOS **Release** builds also green. Audit ledger updated (T91-RF1/RF2 resolutions, Area Summary rows, verdict 4 → 2 open P1). **Not touched (out of scope per prompt):** T91-RF3 (account-switch write race) — explicitly deferred to the next prompt. **Minimal live-AO3 check remaining:** a real ambiguous reply (kill connectivity mid-POST) against a parent thread seeded on a later work-comments page, confirming the standalone-thread fetch finds it and no duplicate posts; write actions remain never-live-tested by an agent, per existing project policy. **Follow-up (2026-07-13, review hand-back):** a second-pass review found two residual guard-escape paths, both closed same day. **P1:** `CommentsModel.reverify` verified the composer's *current* text (editable while `.ambiguous`, Check Again stays visible) instead of the pending submission's actual body — editing then tapping Check Again could authoritatively verify the wrong text, get a spurious `.absent`, and release the block on the real unresolved original (enabling a genuine duplicate on retry). Fixed with a new pure `CommentsModel.verificationTarget(pendingKey:composerContext:)` (deliberately takes no composer-text parameter) that `runVerification` now goes through exclusively, always resolving `pendingKey.normalizedBody`; added `syncSubmissionGuardToComposerText` (called from the composer's text `onChange`, scoped to `.ambiguous` only) so editing away from the blocked text exits that display immediately instead of showing a stale banner. **P2:** `CommentSubmissionKey` carried the requesting screen's `chapterID`, so the same logical reply got different keys (and a disconnected block) from an Inbox focused thread vs. a work-comments screen; fixed by normalizing `chapterID` out of the key in `CommentSubmissionKey.init` (both endpoints are chapter-agnostic). Also hardened: `resolveAmbiguity(.unknown)` no longer risks a stuck `.verifying` with a nil `pendingKey`; `verifyCommentPosted`'s standalone-thread branch now requires the fetched page to actually contain the requested parent before trusting an `.absent`. **Tests (+6):** chapter-scope key equality (replies + top-level), cross-scope block enforcement, `verificationTarget` correctness (ignores composer text, nil-pendingKey case, found-resolves-success), and the exact edit-then-Check-Again regression repro proving only the original key resolves while a distinct edited key stays independently postable. **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN — **452 tests / 47 suites**, iOS + macOS Debug + Release builds green. Audit ledger's T91-RF2 resolution note amended with this follow-up. |
| T-94 | **Reader completion and macOS progress** — close release-blocking findings A7-F1 (iOS marks a work finished at 99% totalProgression and may free its EPUB before true completion) and A7-F2 (macOS never persists or restores intra-chapter reading position) from `docs/RELEASE_READINESS_FABLE5.md`. | Claude | `release-fixes` (developed on `claude/reader-completion-progress-fa8b77`) | ✅ DONE | **A7-F1:** removed the `>= 0.99` shortcut from `ReadiumReaderView.openBook`'s locator callback; progress persistence and completion are now separate signals. True end = the navigator's own last-resource/end state via Readium 3.9.0's `ViewportObservingNavigator`: new pure `ReadiumReaderCompletion.isAtEnd` (`Features/ReaderReadium/ReadiumReaderCompletion.swift`) requires the final reading-order resource visible with its intra-resource trailing edge at **exactly 1.0** — the value Readium clamps to at a resource's end and itself compares `== 1.0` against — so no float tolerance can re-admit the defect; `totalProgression` (the *leading* edge, inexactly interpolated) is never consulted. `ReadiumBook` implements `navigator(_:viewportDidChange:)` and fires rising-edge `onReachedPublicationEnd`; the view finishes only `isComplete` works there (WIPs stay manual); `.onDisappear`'s `freeEPUBIfFinished` stays gated on that verified `isFinished` + unchanged `isProtected`. **A7-F2:** the legacy layout script now maintains one normalized semantic position for both modes (`window.__fraction` = content fraction at the viewport's leading edge: `scrollY/scrollHeight` scrolled, `page/total` paged), posts it rAF-throttled, re-derives page/scroll from it in `applyPaged`/`applyScroll` (mode switches, style reflows, and a now-both-modes resize handler preserve the spot), and exposes `readerRestore(fraction)` invoked post-`didFinish`+inject. Every script message carries a per-load `gen`; `ReaderController` increments it per load and platform-neutral `ReaderBridgeMessage.parse` (`Features/Reader/ReaderProgressBridge.swift`) drops stale-generation messages (old-chapter callbacks can never overwrite current state; paged page/total and scroll-bottom events are newly gated too). Persistence reuses the existing `lastSpineIndex` + previously reader-unused `lastScrollFraction` (both already in backups + folder sync), written through `ReaderProgressBridge`'s debounce (≥2s between streamed writes, 0.001 noise floor) with guaranteed flushes on dismissal (+explicit save), chapter transitions (spine+fraction as one pair), mode/style transitions, and `NSApplication.willTerminate`. Chapter revisits restore a per-session remembered fraction (A→B→A lands at A's spot); explicit same-chapter re-pick resets to top; backward paging keeps last-page landing; `WorkReaderPreparation.restoreReadableEPUB` clears the fraction with the spine so re-downloads can't restore stale positions. **Tests (25 new):** `ReadiumReaderTests` +7 completion cases (0.99 / 0.999 / mid-final-resource don't complete; true end does; non-final end doesn't; two-resource spread; nil guards); new `WorkLifecycleCompletionTests` (4: unfinished at 0.99/0.999 keeps EPUB → offline reopen intact; protected finished keeps EPUB; unprotected finished frees); new `ReaderProgressBridgeTests` (14: stale-gen gating, message shapes, exact paged round-trips incl. totals 3/7/313, containing-page mapping, proportional reflow remap, mode-switch fixed point, A→B→A memory, seeded reopen, same-chapter reset, debounce/noise/flush). **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN — invariants, lint (pre-existing warnings only), full iOS suite **428 tests / 47 suites** (iPhone 17 / iOS 26.5), macOS build, whitespace; **iOS and macOS Release builds** green. Audit ledger updated (A7-F1/A7-F2 resolutions, Area 7 rows, verdict 6 → 4 open P1). **Still owed (manual gate, unchanged):** real-device/simulator read-throughs — finish a real work at its true end (iOS), macOS scrolled/paged midpoint reopen + mode-switch + resize + A→B→A + quit-relaunch. |
| T-92 | **Backup and EPUB archive integrity hardening** — close release-blocking findings A2-F1 (stale-archive tag clobber), A5-F2 (unsafe MiniZip archive input), and A5-F3 (unvalidated backup EPUB replacement) from `docs/RELEASE_READINESS_FABLE5.md`. | Claude | `claude/backup-archive-integrity-fd21df` | ✅ DONE | **A2-F1:** `KudosBackupService.restore`'s per-work tag merge (`KudosBackup.swift`) no longer replaces `work.tags`; it unions in only archived tag names missing locally (exact `Tag.name` match) and never removes a tag absent from a stale archive — there's no per-tag tombstone, so removal is never inferred from absence. **A5-F2:** `MiniZip.swift` rewritten around a throwing `init(data:) throws` (was failable `init?`): checked-arithmetic bounds on every central-directory/local-header range, signature checks, stored-vs-declared size consistency, an allow-list of method 0/8 with the encrypted-flag bit rejected, and per-entry (200 MB) / cumulative (500 MB) / compression-ratio (1100:1) limits — all enforced before any `subdata` or `Data(count:)` allocation. `unzip(to:)` normalizes/rejects unsafe entry names (absolute, `..`, backslash, drive/scheme-like) and extracts into a **fresh staging directory** first, replacing the real destination only after every entry validates — a hostile archive can never leave partial or unsafe output behind. `EPUB.swift`/`WorkImporter.swift` updated for the throwing initializer (`try?`, no behavior change for valid EPUBs). **A5-F3:** `KudosBackupService.restore` now stages every incoming EPUB blob and preflights it through the same hardened validator `ReadingQueueService.replaceEPUB` already uses for AO3-download/user-import replacement (`EPUBDocument.inspectPackage`, backed by the hardened MiniZip), so backup and folder-sync restore (which both funnel through this one function) share the identical safe path. Policy: invalid assets are **skipped with a recoverable result** (new `KudosBackupRestoreSummary.skippedInvalidEPUBs`, surfaced in `conflictMessage`) rather than failing the whole restore transaction; the existing file, `hasEPUB`, and preservation state are left untouched. **Tests:** new `KudosTests/MiniZipHostileTests.swift` (11 hand-assembled hostile-ZIP fixtures — traversal, absolute/backslash/drive-letter paths, truncated record, oversized declared size, implausible ratio, cumulative-size limit, encrypted entry, unsupported method, plus a valid-EPUB round trip); `KudosBackupTests.swift` gained `staleArchiveWithoutTagsDoesNotRemoveNewerLocalTag`, `repeatedRestoreOfArchiveTagsIsIdempotent`, `invalidBackupEPUBLeavesValidLocalEPUBUnchanged`, `tombstoneSuppressedWorkDoesNotWriteEPUBBlob`; `FolderSyncTests.swift` gained `syncDownRejectsInvalidEPUBWithoutOverwritingLocalCopy` (proves the shared validation path end-to-end). The two prior positive backup tests that used arbitrary strings (`"restored-epub"`, `"queued-epub"`) as EPUB bytes now use the real minimal `KudosTests/Fixtures/sample.epub`. **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` invariants/lint green (no new warnings); full iOS suite **387 tests / 43 suites** green (`iPhone 17`, iOS 26.5); macOS build green; whitespace clean. Audit ledger (`docs/RELEASE_READINESS_FABLE5.md`) updated: A2-F1/A5-F2/A5-F3 marked resolved with evidence, Executive Verdict/Area Summary/Open Findings counts updated (11 → 8 open P1). **Not touched (out of scope per task):** A5-F1 (cookie isolation), A5-F4 (Keychain deletion), A2-F2 (P3 stale-but-valid blob overwrite), backup v7 schema, reader architecture. **Follow-up (2026-07-13, code review hand-back):** found and closed a validator/extractor asymmetry — entry-name safety was only checked during `unzip` (extraction), not `MiniZip.init` (validation), so `EPUBDocument.inspectPackage` (the A5-F3 preflight) could pass a structurally-valid EPUB carrying one extra, unsafely-named entry the OPF never referenced; `replaceEPUB` would overwrite a valid local EPUB with it, only failing later at real open time on the macOS legacy reader. Fixed by validating every entry name during `init`, matching extraction; bundled the same-cause duplicate-entry-name divergence (`data(named:)` first-match vs `unzip` last-wins) into a rejection too. New shared `KudosTests/HostileZipFixture.swift` (extracted from `MiniZipHostileTests`, now also used by `KudosBackupTests`); new tests `structurallyValidEPUBWithUnreferencedHostileEntryIsRejected`, `duplicateEntryNameIsRejected`, and `backupEPUBWithUnreferencedHostileEntryLeavesValidLocalEPUBUnchanged` (proves the exact scenario end-to-end through real `KudosBackupService.restore`). Re-verified: invariants/lint clean, full iOS suite **390 tests / 43 suites** green, macOS build green (both independently re-run this pass, not just claimed). |
| T-93 | **Authentication isolation and logout hardening** — close release-blocking findings A5-F1 (anonymous AO3 reads inherited the shared authenticated cookie jar) and A5-F4 (Keychain deletion failures were suppressed while the UI reported logout) from `docs/RELEASE_READINESS_FABLE5.md`. | Claude | `claude/auth-isolation-logout-dc9aae` | ✅ DONE | **A5-F1:** `AO3Client`'s single session (`AO3Client.swift:23-49`, new `makeAnonymousSessionConfiguration()`) now runs with `httpCookieStorage = nil`, `httpShouldSetCookies = false`, `httpCookieAcceptPolicy = .never` instead of `URLSessionConfiguration.default` — it can no longer read or write any cookie jar, so a signed-in session's cookie can never ride along on a nominally anonymous search/browse/tag request. `AO3CookieBridge.install`/`clearAO3Cookies` (`AO3SessionVault.swift:295-321`) stopped mirroring into `HTTPCookieStorage.shared` (the very store the anonymous client used to read from) — only WebKit's own cookie store is touched now, which is all login/browse WebViews ever needed. The authenticated coalescer's key is now the extracted, testable `AO3Client.authCoalescingKey(url:cookieHeader:)`; it was already URL+Cookie-scoped per account and structurally separate from the anonymous URL-only coalescer, so closing the cookie leak closes the account-bridging risk too. **A5-F4:** `CascadingAO3SessionVault.delete()` (`AO3SessionVault.swift:218-292`) always attempts both the Keychain and file deletes now (the old flow re-threw a Keychain failure before ever calling `file.delete()`); both stores are protocol-typed for test injection. New non-secret `AO3SessionRemovalTracking` flag (`UserDefaultsAO3SessionRemovalTracker`) is set whenever a durable delete fails and cleared on success (or a fresh login). `AO3AuthService.logout()` still clears all soft/live state (memory, WebKit/HTTP cookies, username hint) unconditionally, but only reports "Logged out of AO3." when the vault delete actually succeeds — on failure it surfaces a retryable notice instead, now visible on `PrivacyDataView` right where the user tapped Remove AO3 Session. `restoreSession()` checks the pending flag before ever calling `vault.load()`, so a session that couldn't be durably deleted can never be restored on relaunch, no matter what it contains; the same `try?`-free semantics apply to the expiration-cleanup path. **Tests:** `CascadingAO3SessionVaultTests` (4 cases: both-stores-attempted, both-succeed, missing-entitlement-ignored, file-only-failure-surfaced), `AO3CookieBridgeTests.installNeverWritesToTheSharedHTTPCookieStorage`, `AO3ClientPolicyTests` gained 3 cookie/coalescing-scope cases, and `AO3AuthServiceTests` gained 3 cases (`logoutSurfacesRetryableFailureInsteadOfClaimingSuccess`, `pendingRemovalRefusesToRestoreStaleSessionOnRelaunch`, `pendingRemovalRetrySucceedsAndClearsPendingState`) plus every existing case updated to inject an isolated in-memory removal tracker (avoids `UserDefaults.standard` cross-test leakage). **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN — invariants, lint (warnings only, pre-existing, none in touched files), **382 tests / 44 suites**, iOS + macOS builds, whitespace clean. **Not covered (documented, out of scope):** `AO3Client.downloadEPUB` has no authenticated variant, so a "registered users only" restricted work's EPUB download — which previously could only succeed by riding the now-removed ambient cookie — now behaves like any anonymous request; adding an authenticated download path is a separate feature change. Signed-device Keychain verification (a real locked/unavailable Keychain) remains a manual gate. This branch was developed from pre-T-92 `main` (37987dd) as a sibling of T-92's `claude/backup-archive-integrity-fd21df`; both are now reconciled together in this merge into `release-fixes`. **Follow-up fix (same day, review-caught upgrade gap):** the initial pass removed both the write and delete of `HTTPCookieStorage.shared` but never purged what a pre-fix, signed-in build had already left there — a persistent "remember me" cookie with nothing left in the app to ever delete it, still live-reachable via `AO3AuthorAvatar`'s `AsyncImage` (`URLSession.shared`, same default cookie jar) against guaranteed AO3-host avatar URLs. Added `AO3CookieBridge.purgeLegacySharedCookieJar()` (`AO3SessionVault.swift`). **Second follow-up (same day, review-caught ordering race):** the purge was first called from the top of `restoreSession()`, but that only runs from the root view's `.task`, and SwiftUI gives no ordering guarantee between sibling `.task`s — a sibling avatar's own `AsyncImage` fetch could in principle win the race on the very first post-upgrade launch and carry the cookie once before the purge ran. Moved the call into `AO3AuthService.init()` instead: `auth` is a `@State` initializer (`ContentView.swift:23`), which SwiftUI always evaluates before that view's `body` — and therefore before any `.task` in the tree — can run, closing the race structurally. Test renamed/strengthened to `initPurgesLegacyCookieFromTheSharedHTTPCookieStorageBeforeAnyTaskCanRace`, now asserting the cookie is gone immediately after construction, before `restoreSession()` is ever called. Re-verified both times: `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN, **384 tests / 44 suites**, iOS + macOS builds. **Regression found 2026-07-14 (T-100):** disabling cookie handling outright (`httpCookieStorage = nil`, `.never`) also discarded Cloudflare's own `cf_clearance`/`__cf_bm` cookies, so every request looked like a brand-new client and AO3 started serving a CAPTCHA/managed challenge; separately, it silently dropped the `Set-Cookie` on a write's redirect (otwarchive re-signs `_otwarchive_session` there to carry the flash message via Rails' `CookieStore`), which a later Comments-write-path change's honest-success-reading logic (T-99) exposed as false "couldn't confirm" outcomes. See T-100 for the fix (private ephemeral cookie jar for Cloudflare's cookies only, plus a redirect-cookie relay for the auth cookie) — A5-F1's actual invariant (the auth cookie never lives in shared/ambient state) is preserved throughout. |
| T-91 | **AO3 Inbox v2** — native bulk read/unread/delete, filter/sort controls, and current-page select mode using AO3's parsed inbox forms. | Codex | `inbox-refinement` | 🔄 IN PROGRESS | Parsed fixture-backed bulk/filter forms and implemented current-page selection/actions. Inbox cards share Comments' Guest/User/Author/Me chips, paced avatars, Reply capsule, green trailing-check Replied badge, and the same ellipsis control. Me resolves by canonical account path and wins over Author; Author resolves coauthors/alternate pseuds by canonical creator identity without pseud-name or guest false positives; explicit Anonymous Creator remains provenance-gated. Card taps open the bounded Parent Thread-or-self view; Chapter opens the real chapter page and focuses the exact root; Reply now loads that same native focused thread and opens its existing composer. Ellipsis actions are thread/chapter, copy link, parsed single-item Mark Read/Unread, and confirmed Delete From Inbox; Reply stays exclusively in its dedicated capsule. Because official Inbox markup omits creators and work stats, Activity › Inbox now hydrates only unique work ids on the visible page: local/profile/auth-scoped cache first, then sequential paced authenticated metadata requests with view-owned cancellation, systemic-failure stop, and a capped auth-scoped cache. That one full `AO3CommentsWorkContext` updates badges automatically and supplies title/creator/fandom/rating/chapters to the destination summary card. Writes use a scope-owned operation token across account resets and invalidate every cached Inbox page/filter for the originating auth scope after success. **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN — invariants, lint gate (warnings only), **371 tests / 42 suites**, iOS + macOS builds, whitespace; focused adversarial follow-ups closed identity, navigation/focus, visible-page lifecycle, stale-cache, account-switch write-guard, Reply, and non-work routing blockers. ---

## 🅿️ Backlog (prioritized)

### P1 — remaining
- **T-07 · Lazy / on-demand chapter extraction** 🅿️ *deferred.* Legacy/macOS reader
  only (the iOS Readium reader is already lazy); correctness-risky (WKWebView must
  resolve each chapter's CSS/image/font resources). Low ROI — do only if large-work
  perf becomes a real problem. Target: legacy `EPUBDocument.open` upfront-unzip.

### P1 — non-EPUB community-copy import (T-152 series; owner-approved 2026-07-30)
Rationale and the whole-series design live in T-152's In-Progress row — read it first.
Owner decisions, settled: **convert everything to EPUB** rather than teach the app new
formats ("reflowable, open, easy to render and manipulate"); **always keep the original
bytes** since a converted last-known-copy is lossy; **prefer tested FOSS over
hand-rolling** — "as many dependencies as are necessary". PDF is explicitly *in* scope
(the owner overruled deferring it: converters exist, so convert).
- **T-153 · DOCX + PDF converters — bundled JS and native frameworks only, no C toolchain.**
  **DOCX → [mammoth.js](https://github.com/mwilliamson/mammoth.js) (BSD-2) run in
  JavaScriptCore.** `mammoth.browser.js` is one self-contained file, so this adds a
  resource, not a build target. It maps Word's *semantic* styles (Heading 1 → `h1`)
  instead of copying formatting, which is exactly right for reflow — and it replaces the
  ~120-line `word/document.xml` parser this series originally planned. Its own docs warn
  it performs **no sanitisation**, so its output must go through T-152's SwiftSoup
  `Cleaner` pass. **PDF → PDFKit + Vision, no dependency.** Researched and rejected as
  unembeddable-on-iOS: calibre's `ebook-convert` (Python), poppler's `pdftohtml`
  (desktop C++ with fontconfig/freetype), and `pdf2htmlEX` (emits *fixed*-layout EPUB,
  which defeats the entire point of converting). Native path: `PDFDocument` page
  `attributedString` for born-digital text, `VNRecognizeTextRequest` for scans. Lossy by
  nature — acceptable **only** because T-152 keeps the original bytes.
- **T-154 · Kindle + solid-archive formats — the one task that adds C dependencies.**
  **MOBI/AZW3/AZW/AZW4/PRC/PDB → [libmobi](https://github.com/bfabiszewski/libmobi)
  (LGPL-3, compatible with this app's GPL-3).** Its own `mobitool -e` already emits EPUB,
  so the conversion path is *in* the library — this is why the format tier moved from
  "refuse, tell the user to run calibre" to "feasible". **RAR/7z → libarchive (BSD, RAR
  reader imported from The Unarchiver) or unarr (LGPL-3).** Both sidestep RARLAB's
  non-free `unrar` entirely. Rarest formats in forum distribution, so this is last:
  it is the only task needing vendored C + an SPM target in a project that currently has
  exactly two pure-Swift packages (SwiftSoup, Readium).
- **T-155 · Provenance, `.kudosbackup` **v9**, and the app's first manual metadata editor.**
  ⚠️ **v9, not v8** — v8 is already taken by `33349be7` (in-book annotations:
  bookmarks/highlights/notes, `ReadingAnnotation` + `KudosBackupAnnotation`); this branch
  now sits on that tip, so `currentVersion` reads 8 here. New `SavedWork` fields:
  `sourceSite`, `importedFormat`, `originalFileName`, `originalSHA256`. **Free identity
  win from the research pass:** FanFicFare — which produced most fanfic circulating as
  EPUB — stamps `dc:identifier` = `fanficfare-uid:<site>-u<authorID>-s<storyID>`,
  `dc:source` = the story URL, and `dc:publisher` = the site name into every file it
  writes. So a non-AO3 story's site and ID are **read, not guessed**, which also repairs
  dedup for non-AO3 works — today it falls back to title + author + exact file size
  ([`WorkImporter.swift:493`](kudos-ao3-reader/Services/WorkImporter.swift:493)), which
  breaks the moment two people re-encode the same fic. Plus a WorkDetail provenance badge
  ("Community copy · fanfiction.net · from Chapter1.docx"), a manifest bump to v9 with a
  `PersistenceMigrationService` stage, and an **Edit Details** sheet — nothing in the app
  can edit a work's title today (only the backup merge and the AO3 refresh write
  `work.title`), so without it every community import keeps its junk filename forever.
  Two existing invariants already work in this feature's favour and must not be broken:
  `isProtected` is true when `ao3WorkID == nil`
  ([`Models.swift:357`](kudos-ao3-reader/Models/Models.swift:357)) so community copies
  never have their file freed, and `refreshFromAO3` no-ops without an AO3 id
  ([`WorkTags.swift:19`](kudos-ao3-reader/Services/WorkTags.swift:19)) so FFN works never
  hammer AO3.

### P2 — features (roadmap "Later" list; ID assigned on pickup)
- **Library Sync Folder hardening** — folder-based sync is implemented without
  CloudKit entitlements; remaining work is real-device iCloud Drive verification,
  conflict UX polish, and any follow-up from large-library testing.
- **Highlights / notes / annotations** — large; a reader annotation system.
- **Text-to-Speech (TTS)** — large; `AVSpeechSynthesizer` in the reader.

### Phase 3 — native AO3 writes (most fragile; needs the per-page CSRF token)
- **Leave kudos · comment · subscribe · AO3-side bookmark** — fetch the work/page,
  extract its `authenticity_token`, POST the form, detect a login redirect. Build on
  `AO3AuthService.authenticatedRequest`. See [[native-ao3-roadmap]] Phase 3 +
  [`docs/AO3Authentication.md`](docs/AO3Authentication.md).

### UI-refinement & maintainability pass (user-planned — *after* the feature backlog)

- **🔴 Live AO3 verification (HIGH).** Every scraping feature shipped 2026-06-20

  verified only — never run with a real AO3 session.** Selector/URL assumptions are

  (AO3 sub-picker, Library bulk-select, download-queue banner, About page, Continue
  Reading shelf). All build/test-verified and the app boots clean, but unseen.
- **Keychain on a signed device build.** Confirm the session actually persists to
  Keychain on real hardware (the WebKit-store fallback is meant to be the
  Simulator/dev backstop only). See [`docs/AO3Authentication.md`](docs/AO3Authentication.md).
- **Single-branch consolidation (2026-06-24).** The `main` (legacy) / `readium-migration`
  split was merged into a **single `main` branch**; all other branches were deleted
  (local + remote). The old cross-branch hazards once listed here no longer apply — no
  scratch worktrees, no "pollution stashed on main", and `main` now *legitimately*
  includes Readium (iOS), so a `readiumLocator` reference is correct, **not** a stale
  build artifact. The legacy WKWebView reader is now `#if os(macOS)`-guarded (iOS uses
  the Readium navigator). Still: build **both** iOS (Readium) and macOS (legacy) before
  claiming a cross-platform change is done.

| T-145 | **ZIP64 + streaming `.kudosbackup` export (T-139 follow-on: size ceiling removed)** — the two limits T-139 left in place are gone. `MiniZip` reader now has full ZIP64 support: the central-directory locator follows the ZIP64 EOCD locator + record when present, per-entry sizes/offsets saturated at classic sentinels resolve through the 0x0001 extended-information extra field (`parseZip64Extra`, `safeU64`), and a saturated field with no ZIP64 record to resolve it **rejects the archive** — sentinels are never silently read as values; all ZIP64 values pass `Int(exactly:)` plus the same method/size/ratio/limit validation as classic entries. Writer side: new `MiniZip.ArchiveWriter` streams each entry's local header + payload to a closure sink (file handle or in-memory buffer) as appended, holding only central-directory records until `finish()`; file-backed entries are read twice in bounded chunks (incremental `crc32Update` pass, then payload pass) with strict byte-count verification, and ZIP64 local/central extras + ZIP64 EOCD record/locator are emitted **exactly when** a value outgrows its classic field — smaller archives stay byte-identical to the old writer (`archiveData` is now the writer with an in-memory sink). Export path: `KudosBackupService.makeExportPlan` (main-actor snapshot: encoded manifest + asset URLs, `zipData()`-identical ordering) + nonisolated `writeArchive` stream the archive to a temp file off the main actor — **constant memory at any library size**, unreadable assets skipped leniently, partial file removed on failure; Settings presents the item-based `fileExporter` over a `Transferable` file wrapper (spinner while preparing, temp cleaned on completion/cancellation). `Limits.backup` raised to **250k entries / 1 GB per entry / 64 GB total** (restore still materializes assets per entry while merging — streaming restore is a documented deferral). | T-100 | **Restore AO3/Cloudflare cookie continuity without reintroducing A5-F1** — regression from T-93: `AO3Client`'s anonymous session (`httpCookieStorage = nil`, `httpCookieAcceptPolicy = .never`) can no longer accept or resend Cloudflare's `cf_clearance`/`__cf_bm` cookies, so every request looks like a brand-new client and AO3 now serves a CAPTCHA/managed challenge. **Fix:** `makeAnonymousSessionConfiguration` (`AO3Client.swift`) switches the session to `URLSessionConfiguration.ephemeral` (a private, in-memory, per-process cookie jar automatically distinct from `HTTPCookieStorage.shared`) with `httpCookieAcceptPolicy = .always`/`httpShouldSetCookies = true`, so anonymous reads keep Cloudflare's `cf_clearance`/`__cf_bm` warm across requests. New `AO3Client.purgeSessionCookie` deletes any `AO3RequestDefaults.sessionCookieName` (`_otwarchive_session`, now single-sourced) cookie from that jar after every anonymous fetch, so the jar can never carry account identity even transiently — the actual A5-F1 invariant (auth cookie never in shared/ambient state) is unchanged; authenticated/write requests still build their `Cookie` header explicitly from `AO3AuthService`'s own persisted per-account `AO3Session`, never from this jar, so two accounts' requests can never race over one cookie value. New `AO3Client.challengeCookieHeader`/`AO3AuthService.mergedCookieHeader` let authenticated/write requests *read-only* append the jar's current Cloudflare cookies onto their explicit header too, so they aren't left un-cleared while anonymous traffic is. **Also fixes the CAA-2 redirect regression found by Part A's (T-99) adversarial review:** new `AO3RedirectCookieRelay` (`URLSessionTaskDelegate`, stateless, task-scoped) restores a same-host write redirect's fresh `_otwarchive_session` value (which carries otwarchive's success/error flash via Rails' `CookieStore`) onto the followed request, without ever touching shared/ambient cookie storage — wired into `performAuthenticatedFetch`/`submitWrite` via `session.data(for:delegate:)`. **Tests (+14):** private-ephemeral-jar-not-shared invariant (replaces T-93's now-obsolete `anonymousSessionConfigurationDisablesCookieHandling` pin), Cloudflare-cookie filtering excludes the auth cookie (present + auth-only-empty cases), purge removes only the auth cookie / tolerates nil storage, redirect-relay merge (replace/append/no-op/nil-header/nil-url/multi-cookie cases), auth+challenge header concatenation (present/absent/empty). **Verified:** `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN — invariants, lint, iOS **486 tests / 50 suites** (iPhone 17/iOS 26.5), macOS build, whitespace. **Not done:** no live AO3 request by an agent (project policy) — owner must confirm the CAPTCHA stops appearing and that a real edit/delete no longer shows "couldn't confirm." **Docs:** T-93's row annotated with this regression + fix; T-99's row and the audit report's CAA-2 status note reference this fix. **Adversarial review + follow-up fix (2026-07-14):** a review of this same commit found a genuine, CONFIRMED bug in `AO3RedirectCookieRelay`: it attached the merged Cookie header — including the freshly re-signed, live `_otwarchive_session` value — onto whatever host the redirect's `newRequest` targeted, with no check that the target was still an AO3 host. A cross-host redirect (open-redirect parameter, misconfigured route, or an intermediary domain such as a Cloudflare challenge subdomain that happens to echo a same-named cookie) could have carried the real account session cookie off-site — reopening A5-F1 through an explicit per-request header, outside every cookie-storage protection this task otherwise added. Fixed by a new `RedirectCookieAction` three-way decision (`.leaveUnchanged` / `.set(String)` / `.strip`) in `redirectCookieAction(currentHeader:responseHeaderFields:responseURL:newRequestURL:)`: it checks `AO3RequestDefaults.isTrustedURL(newRequestURL)` first and returns `.strip` for any non-AO3 (or non-HTTPS, or nil) target — actively removing the Cookie header rather than merely leaving it as Foundation built it. **Tests (+6):** cross-host strip, non-HTTPS strip, nil-target strip, same-host set (incl. an AO3 subdomain), same-host no-op when nothing needs updating. Re-verified: `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN, **492 tests / 50 suites**. (A second review finding — `AO3AuthService.swift` crossing SwiftLint's 700-line `file_length` *warning* threshold at 707 — is not a behavioral defect; matches the same non-blocking finding on T-99's own review and needs no action.) **Second follow-up review (2026-07-14):** re-checked the cross-host fix plus the previously-incomplete cross-account-isolation concern (its prior review pass hit a transient API error and never returned a verdict). Cross-host fix confirmed correct; no critic findings. Two confirmed-but-minor gaps in `performFetch`/`downloadEPUB`: `purgeSessionCookie` sat after the throwing `Self.check(response)`, so a non-2xx anonymous response skipped the purge, and `downloadEPUB` never called it at all. Traced impact: harmless in practice — `challengeCookieHeader` unconditionally filters the auth cookie by name regardless of what's in the jar, and authenticated/write requests never read this jar at all, so no leak was ever possible even with the gap — but it contradicted the doc's "structural invariant" claim. Fixed by moving the purge into a `defer` (runs regardless of `check`'s outcome) and adding the identical call to `downloadEPUB`. No new test (the purge's own removal logic is already unit-tested; this was a call-site/ordering fix only). Re-verified: `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN, **492 tests / 50 suites**. Review loop closed — ready for merge consideration. | Claude | `claude/ao3-cookie-policy-3886f9` | `dec87d6`,`8bcca45`,`7234cb2` | 2026-07-14 |

Verified: `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN (**274 tests / 33 suites**, iOS + macOS). **Diagnosed + fixed a related but app-wide bug found while live-testing:** the login sheet's ~10s freeze right after opening was WebKit's one-time WebContent/Networking process spin-up (worse on Simulator + ad-hoc "Sign to Run Locally" signing), triggered the moment `AO3LoginView` first mounted its hidden webview into a real window — the sheet's own presentation animation completes instantly, unrelated to WebKit. New `WebKitPrewarmView` (`kudos-ao3-reader/App/ContentView.swift`) mounted invisibly at the app root warms a throwaway `WKWebView` (loading local `about:blank`, no network) 500ms after first render, so cold launch never competes with it; it never touches `auth.loginWebView` itself so it can't race the real login flow — WebKit's process spin-up is shared process-wide, so this also warms the in-app Browse tab's webview for free. Verified: `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN (**274 tests / 33 suites**, iOS + macOS). **Thread-geometry round** (owner task doc: card-within-card, not timeline): the connector was a far-left rail detached from avatars with reply avatars inside the bubbles. New `CommentThreadGeometry` enforces `connectorX == parentAvatar.centerX == childAvatar.centerX` — one shared 38pt avatar column per thread (parent 38 / reply 30 avatars, all centers x=33), single straight quaternary line drawn by the group-row modifier as arrival (row top → avatar center, behind the opaque-backed circle) + continuation (below avatar → row bottom) segments that join flush across rows; depth ≥2 indents the bubble only, so no elbows exist; action row reordered Reply-left / overflow-right per the doc. One file (`CommentThreadRow.swift`), view-layer only; flat lazy rows + all functional behavior preserved. Verified: `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN (**274 tests / 33 suites**, iOS + macOS). **Avatar-badge round** (owner provided a closer mockup crop after the thread-geometry pass): a reply's avatar now overlaps its own bubble's top-left corner (half poking above/outside, half over the interior) instead of sitting inline inside the bubble — positioned via `.overlay(alignment: .topLeading) + .offset`, a pure visual overlap that doesn't affect bubble layout. Bubble gained real trailing/bottom margin (floating-card look). Hairline separator before the overflow menu shown in the mockup intentionally NOT implemented, per owner. **Adversarial review** (workflow `wf_799cad23-a82`, 4 lenses + independent verification) caught 2 real regressions before landing: (1) `continuationTop(forDepth: 0)` used a center-anchor formula (`parentAvatarSize/2`) for the top-anchored parent avatar (no offset trick, unlike the reply avatar) — line started 19pt early, bleeding through the parent avatar's lower half; fixed to the full `parentAvatarSize`, and restored the parent avatar's opaque backing (dropped when parent/reply code paths split). (2) reply avatar's corner offset was applied before the depth-based bubble indent, so at depth ≥ 2 it drifted right with the bubble instead of staying on the fixed centerline as documented, leaving the connector dangling; fixed by having the offset subtract `bubbleIndent(forDepth:)` so the avatar's absolute x is depth-invariant (documented residual: past depth 2 the avatar detaches from its own bubble's corner, not from the line). Verified: `Scripts/verify.sh` ALL GREEN (**274 tests / 33 suites**, iOS + macOS).  **Avatar-badge round** (owner mockup: corner-overlap avatar) landed as `96b1827`, then an adversarial review (workflow, 4 lenses) found 2 real high-severity bugs before any owner testing: the parent-avatar connector bled through the lower half of placeholder/guest avatars (continuationTop used half the avatar's radius instead of its full height), and the reply avatar drifted off the fixed connector line at nesting depth >= 2, leaving the arrival segment dangling in blank space — both confirmed via independent re-verification. **Before those fixes could land, the owner rejected the whole approach**: "profile picture must remain inside bubble." Reverted to a plain avatar-then-content row (matching the parent's own layout) inside a bubble with real margin on every side — a genuinely floating card, not the corner-overlap treatment. Carried forward the two real fixes since they're not overlap-specific (parent connector now reaches the avatar's true bottom edge; replies no longer thread the connector through their own avatar at all — the arrival segment only needs each bubble's own top edge, and a non-last reply's continuation just runs the row's full height, relying on the bubble's own opaque fill to hide whatever falls behind it, sidestepping variable AO3-comment-length bubble heights entirely). A second adversarial-review attempt partially failed on a usage-limit mid-run (2/5 agents completed, refuting its 2 findings; one review lens never ran) — rather than re-launching a third workflow, did a direct, own-hand arithmetic check and found one more real gap: `bubbleIndent`'s per-depth increment pushed a bubble's left edge past the connector's fixed x at nesting depth 3+ (46 > 36), leaving the continuation line floating unhidden beside a triply-nested reply; fixed by capping the indent at one step past the first reply (depths 1-2 — the pair explicitly required, first reply + reply-to-reply — were already safely within bounds; only the rare depth-3+ case changes).
