New platform-agnostic `kudos-ao3-reader/Reading/ReaderSection.swift`: `ReaderSectionKind` (`.preface`/`.summary`/`.chapter`/`.afterword`/`.other`) + `ReaderSection` (one per spine item, `id == spineIndex`) + `ReaderSectionBuilder.build(tocEntries:spineHrefs:)`, which reconciles a TOC (however incomplete) against the full spine: classifies by exact-match TOC title ("Preface"/"Summary"/"Afterword"; everything else — "Chapter N", "Epilogue", "Epilogue: Chapter N" — defaults to `.chapter`, satisfying the "treat epilogues as story chapters" requirement for free); synthesizes a `.summary` entry for an untitled spine gap, but **only** when a real `.preface`-classified TOC entry was already seen and no summary has been placed yet — this single gate is the entire AO3-specificity boundary, so an EPUB with no "Preface" TOC entry at all (i.e. every non-AO3 EPUB, verified against the bundled `sample.epub` fixture) can never enter the synthesis branch and is byte-for-byte unaffected. `ReaderSection.pillLabel(storyChapterTotal:)` renders "P"/"S"/"A" for front/back matter and "`<index>`/`<total>`" for real chapters, where `<total>` prefers `SavedWork.totalChapterCount(from:)` (new — parses AO3's own "Chapters: X/Y" stat's Y side, `nil` for "5/?" WIPs) over `[ReaderSection].storyChapterCount` (new — counts only `.chapter`-kind sections), matching the spec's stated preference order exactly. **iOS/Readium** (`Features/ReaderReadium/ReadiumReaderView.swift`): `ReadiumBook` gained `readingOrder`/`sections`, built once in `open()` by resolving `Publication.tableOfContents()`'s `Link.href` strings against `Publication.readingOrder` via a new fragment/path-insensitive `ReaderSectionBuilder.hrefKey` (verified `Link.href: String`/`Link.title: String?` directly against the vendored Readium source rather than inferring the API); `go(toSpineIndex:)` added so the synthesized Summary entry (which has no TOC `Link` of its own) is still navigable through Readium's real navigator. `progressPill` and the `chapterRows` TOC sheet now read `book.sections` instead of raw `book.toc`/`pos.chapter`; per-position page/percent tracking is untouched. **macOS legacy reader** (`Features/Reader/ReaderView.swift`): given full parity rather than being left behind again (the exact bug class T-73 already found once, in a different subsystem) — `EPUBDocument.chapters` already carries pre-resolved spine indices, so wiring here needed no href-matching at all; new `sections` `@State` built alongside `document` in `load()`, `positionLabel`/new `currentSectionLabel`/`longFormPositionLabel` replace the raw `Ch \(currentIndex+1)/\(chapterCount)` computation for both paged and scroll reading modes, `chapterRows` reads `sections` instead of `document?.chapters`. Both platforms filter `.kind != .other` out of their index/TOC sheet (per spec) while leaving `.other` items reachable by ordinary page-turning (page-turn/pagination mechanics were not touched on either platform). | T-75 | **Fix EPUB import silently failing for undownloaded iCloud Drive files** — Settings → Import EPUB let the user select files, but "Open" appeared to do nothing for any file shown with the cloud-download icon in Files. Root cause, confirmed by reading the full call chain (`SettingsView.importEPUBs` → `importUserEPUB` → `UserEPUBInspection.inspect` → `EPUBDocument.inspectPackage`, `kudos-ao3-reader/Reading/EPUB.swift:236`): the chain has zero iCloud-download awareness anywhere — `inspectPackage` does `try? Data(contentsOf: url)` on a `.fileImporter`-returned URL the instant the picker dismisses; a not-yet-materialized placeholder fails that read and `try?` silently converts it to the generic `EPUBError.unreadableFile`, with no wait and no retry, matching the user's own diagnosis exactly ("Files download before being imported and if the select view is dismissed before download is done then the import is cancelled"). Fix: new `waitForUbiquitousDownload(of:pollInterval:timeout:)` (`kudos-ao3-reader/Services/WorkImporter.swift`) — no-ops immediately for any non-ubiquitous (already-local) file via `URLResourceKey.isUbiquitousItemKey`, otherwise calls `FileManager.startDownloadingUbiquitousItem(at:)` and polls `.ubiquitousItemDownloadingStatusKey` (0.3s interval, 120s timeout) until `.current`, throwing a new typed `UserEPUBImportError.iCloudDownloadTimedOut` with an actionable message on timeout. `SettingsView.importEPUBs` now: starts security-scoped access for every selected URL up front and holds it for the whole pass (not just the read — materialization needs the scope too) via `accessedURLs = urls.filter { $0.startAccessingSecurityScopedResource() }`; kicks off `startDownloadingUbiquitousItem` for every selected file immediately (so files later in a multi-select are already downloading by the time their turn comes, instead of each one blocking the next); then per file, awaits the download with progress text ("Waiting for iCloud Drive… (N of M)") before importing. `WorkImporter.swift` picked up a `// swiftlint:disable file_length` (702 lines; matches the existing convention on `SettingsView.swift`/`WorkDetailView.swift`/`KudosBackup.swift` rather than splitting cohesive import-funnel code for a lint threshold). Added `EPUBTests.waitForUbiquitousDownloadNoOpsOnAPlainLocalFile` (asserts the overwhelmingly common case — a plain local file — returns in <1s, not the 5s `pollInterval` used in the test, proving the early-return path rather than an accidental fall-through to polling). | T-73 | **Release hardening — 8 consolidated fixes from the three adversarial reviews** — **(1) Dead "Import EPUB" button fixed:** SettingsView chained four sibling `.fileImporter`s on one Form node and SwiftUI honors only one file-dialog presenter per node, so only the last-attached (sync folder, added by T-68 *after* the EPUB importer — which is exactly when the button died) ever presented. Consolidated into one enum-driven importer (`FileImportKind`: fonts/backup/epub/syncFolder; derived `isPresented` binding; per-kind content types/multi-select/handlers preserved exactly, `.fileExporter` untouched); hardened so the completion falls back to a `lastPresentedImport` recorded via `.onChange` — whether dismissal nils the active kind before or after the completion runs is an OS implementation detail, and the result must never be dropped either way. **(2) Sync-up can no longer destroy the only cloud copy:** `coordinatedWriteContents` staged remove-then-write; now stages to a temp and swaps via `replaceItemAt` (moveItem on first write), original package surviving any failed write — staging in `itemReplacementDirectory` (same volume as the destination) so external-drive sync folders don't fail the swap with EXDEV. **(3) Unchanged sync-downs skipped:** `performSyncDown` early-returns (`FolderSyncResult.skippedUnchanged`) when the package's coordinated `contentModificationDate` matches the stamp stored after every successful restore *and* after this device's own sync-up writes — never skipping while unresolved `NSFileVersion` conflicts exist (they can arrive without the main file's date moving). Kills the every-launch full-package read (all EPUB blobs into RAM) for the common nothing-changed case. **(4) TOCTOU duplicate-insert race closed:** `importEPUB` now re-checks `existingWork(forSource:)` after the caller's multi-second download and merges (fill-only, same path as the Recently-Deleted revive, which is byte-identical) into ANY existing match instead of inserting a twin. **(5)** `WorkSearchIndex.rebuildIfNeeded` is async with `Task.yield()` every 200 works + a `modelContext != nil` liveness re-check per record (yields let a user delete a fetched work mid-rebuild; touching an invalidated model asserts — same guard as the migration service). **(6)** Authenticated GETs coalesce in-flight duplicates (`RequestCoalescer` keyed URL+Cookie so a mid-flight account switch can't leak a response across sessions; value carries `response.url?.path` since URLResponse isn't Sendable; login-redirect check unchanged; `submitWrite` untouched — writes never coalesce/retry). **(7)** Locked/failing AO3 works now wait out cooldowns: new `SavedWork.lastTagRefreshAttemptAt` (device-local, deliberately NOT in `.kudosbackup`) gives `needsAO3Refresh` a 24h attempt cooldown (nil/first attempts pass), stamped in `refreshFromAO3` before the fetch; `WorkUpdateChecker` now stamps `lastUpdateCheck` on *failed* checks too so the 6h throttle actually applies to failures. **(8)** Browse batch actions hold their `Task` in `@State` and cancel in `.onDisappear` — the bulk loops' existing CancellationError handling stops them cleanly instead of resolving works after the user leaves. Built via a 7-agent parallel workflow (disjoint file sets) + build stage; the review panel hit a rate limit so the adversarial pass was done by hand, finding 3 hardening gaps (the dismissal-ordering fallback in 1, the EXDEV staging in 2, the liveness guard in 5) — all applied. New tests: failed-sync-up-write preserves the remote package, sync-down skip/re-restore cycle (incl. own-write stamp), importEPUB merges into a non-pending existing match. Verified: `Scripts/lint.sh` clean, `git diff --check` clean, full iOS suite green (198 passed, up from 195), macOS Debug build. **Manual remaining:** simulator tap-through of all four Settings pickers presenting one after another (Fonts → Backup import → EPUB import → Sync Folder) + Export Backup still presenting — the consolidation is logic-verified but the release gate's interactive pass should include it. | Claude | `release-hardening` | (this commit) | 2026-07-07 |
| T-72 | **Library search indexing + AO3 network hardening + storage audit** — three-part scalability/correctness pass. **(1) Local search index:** new derived, rebuildable `SavedWork.searchText` + `searchIndexVersion` (additive fields with defaults — lightweight migration) built by the new `WorkSearchIndex` (normalize = lowercase + diacritic-fold under a fixed `en_US_POSIX` locale, so "Héroïne" matches "heroine" regardless of device locale; composition = title/author/categorized tags/un-categorized flat tags/rating/language/completion words/HTML-stripped summary capped at 600 chars). Explicit `reindex` at every content-mutation chokepoint (`importEPUB` fresh+revive, `importUserEPUB` fresh+duplicate, `WorkTags.refreshFromAO3` + `backfillFromEPUB`, `ReadingQueueService.applyRemoteMetadata`, `WorkMetadataRefresh.refresh`, the backup-restore merge loop) — grep-verified that no other code path writes indexed fields (only model initializers, whose insertion sites all reindex). `rebuildIfNeeded` sweeps `searchIndexVersion != current` at launch (`ContentView`, after migration/sweep) — covers pre-index libraries, older-schema records, and anything a restore missed. Deliberately **not** `markModified()`-driven: a reindex never bumps sync timestamps (it must not look like a user edit to backup/folder-sync merges), and the index never travels in `.kudosbackup` (test-asserted against the manifest JSON) — restore rebuilds it from real fields. Consumers: Global Search's "In Your Library" now matches per-keystroke against the precomputed text (AND-across-terms, so multi-word queries span fields — previously title/author-only substring, re-lowercased per keystroke and per work); `LibraryFilters.matches` now builds its per-work tag `Set`s only for facets that are actually set, so default filtering over a large library costs no per-work set construction and no `tags`-relationship faulting. **(2) AO3 hardening:** User-Agent now appends an honest product token + contact URL (`KudosReader/<CFBundleShortVersionString> (+github.com/cidy02/kudos-ao3-reader)`) to the browser-like base; new slot-claiming `pace()` in `AO3Client` spaces every request start ≥0.6s apart across all four network touchpoints (GET / auth-GET / write-POST / EPUB download, retries included) — the actor's old "one request at a time" header claim was false under actor reentrancy, so the pacing is now real rather than assumed; explicit `AO3Error.forbidden` for HTTP 403 (never retried — hammering a block only prolongs it); fixed a cancellation bug in `FandomWorksView`'s batch loops where a cancelled task burned through all remaining items, counting each instant `CancellationError` as a per-item failure. Pre-existing and verified still sound: `RequestCoalescer` (in-flight GET dedup), `AO3RequestCoordinator` (3-slot concurrency cap), exponential backoff honoring `Retry-After`, typed 429/404/5xx, transient-only retry policy, `WorkUpdateChecker`'s 6-hour per-work throttle. Audited: no `softDelete`/`hardDelete` sits in any network error path — a 404 marks `ao3Unavailable` and keeps the local copy. **(3) Storage audit:** identity/dedup (shared `WorkIdentityIndex`, revive-on-reacquire) and backup round-trip (v7, tombstones, `incomingWins` merges) confirmed sound from this session's earlier passes; EPUB metadata reads already run off-main via `Task.detached` (whole-file reads are inherent to `MiniZip`, acceptable at fanwork sizes); `workTags` flat-union duplication kept deliberately (backup compat + filtering for un-enriched works) but de-duplicated out of the search index at index time. **Known scalability ceiling flagged, not fixed:** every list surface `@Query`s the full `SavedWork` table into memory; fine into the hundreds-to-low-thousands, but the eventual fix (paged/sectioned fetches, or SwiftData `#Index` when the toolchain supports it here) is follow-up work, not this branch. Added `WorkSearchIndexTests` (6: normalization + terms, full-field composition with HTML stripping, AND-matching across fields, fill-path reindex via `applyRemoteMetadata`, launch rebuild once-and-only-once, backup round-trip excludes the index and restore rebuilds it). Verified: `Scripts/lint.sh` clean, `git diff --check` clean, full iOS suite green (195 passed, up from 189), macOS Debug build. | Claude | `indexing-implementation` | (this commit) | 2026-07-06 |
| T-71 | **Canonical Work Identity — one card per AO3 work across Home/Library/Account** — the same AO3 work no longer renders as two separate cards (once local, once remote) on any surface. New shared `WorkIdentityIndex` (the 3-tier matcher: AO3 work ID → canonical AO3 URL → record UUID) extracted from the three previously-independent implementations: `WorkRestoreIndex` (backup/sync restore) is now a thin wrapper over it, and `RemoteWorkContextMenuModifier.existingLocalWork` + `ReadingQueueService.existingWork(for:)` are one-line calls into it — every surface now answers "is this the same work?" identically (`KudosBackupTests` pass unchanged over the refactor, the pure-refactor gate). New `CanonicalWork` (query-time `{local, remote}` pairing with a stable ForEach id) and `CanonicalWorkMerge` with two deliberately distinct operations: **`remoteLed`** for remote-defined lists (Subscriptions, Bookmarks, History, My Works, collection pages — AO3's own order is kept, matched entries carry their local record, local-only works are deliberately absent since not being in the remote list means not being part of it) and **`remoteOnly`** for the hybrid Saved-for-Later surfaces (remote entries whose work exists *anywhere* in the local library drop out entirely — the work already renders as its single richer local card wherever that card lives, e.g. Reading Now). Surfaces updated: Home's Subscriptions carousel renders the new `CanonicalWorkCoverCard` (richer local cover card — reading progress, local context menu, straight-to-reader navigation — when a local record exists; the remote AO3 card otherwise); Library's Saved-for-Later carousel and its See-All `LibrarySectionListView` list dedupe their remote Marked-for-Later entries against the full library (this was the confirmed literal duplicate-rendering bug that motivated the feature); `AO3AccountWorksList` (shared by Bookmarks/History/Marked for Later/My Works/collections + Home's Subscriptions See-All) pairs matched works into `SensitiveWorkRow` local rows. **Privacy:** pairing matches only privacy-visible local works (Home + account lists), so a Hide-mode Mature work keeps its plain remote row instead of surfacing local state for it; in Blur mode the paired local row blurs itself (`SensitiveWorkRow`'s existing behavior). Added `CanonicalWorkMergeTests` (8: ID match; URL-variant match with no stored `ao3WorkID`; remote-only passthrough + local-only dropped; order preservation; duplicate remote mention paired once with unique ForEach ids; record-UUID tier; `CanonicalWork` id/accessor stability). | T-70 | **Preserved Work / 90-day Recently Deleted recovery** — deleting a work, collection, or reading queue now moves it to a recoverable "Recently Deleted" state for 90 days instead of an instant, permanent delete. New `PreservedWorkService` (`softDelete`/`restore`/`hardDelete`/`sweepExpired`) reuses the existing (previously inert) `isDeleted`/`deletedAt` fields plus a new `permanentDeletionScheduledAt: Date?` on `SavedWork`/`WorkCollection`/`ReadingQueue` — deliberately **not** `ReadingQueueMembership` (no user-facing trigger ever soft-deletes a membership alone; removing a work from a queue has always been non-destructive, and a soft-deleted queue's memberships are left untouched so restoring brings the whole queue back intact). `WorkLifecycle.delete` renamed to `hardDelete` (permanent-only, called by `sweepExpired`/"Delete Permanently"); all ~11 call sites across `WorkCardActions`/`LibrarySectionListView`/`LibraryView`/`ReadingQueueService`/`Collections`/`ReadingQueues`/`AccountView`/`PrivacyDataView` now soft-delete instead. Added an AO3-availability check (`SavedWork.ao3Unavailable`, already cached from tag refresh) that escalates the delete-confirmation copy when a work is no longer on AO3 — restoring it becomes the only way to keep it. New `RecentlyDeletedView` (Works/Collections/Reading Queues sections, days-remaining label, swipe-to-restore/delete-permanently) with a Library dashboard entry row shown only when non-empty. Manifest bumped to v7: `permanentDeletionScheduledAt` added to works/collections/queues, and `isDeleted`/`deletedAt` now travel for reading queues for the first time (previously never carried at all); merged the same `incomingWins`-gated way as `isDeleted`. Every existing `@Query` that lists works/collections/queues (12 files) now excludes `isPendingDeletion` so a Recently Deleted record disappears from the normal Library/Home/Search/Account surfaces. `sweepExpired` runs at launch (independent of Auto Sync) and after a successful background sync; a migration pass backfills `permanentDeletionScheduledAt` for any pre-v7 backup that left a record soft-deleted with no recovery deadline. **Two real bugs found and fixed during verification, both worth flagging for future model work:** (1) a property literally named `isDeleted` on an `@Model` class is unreliable — it silently reverts to `false` on every `context.save()`, evidently colliding with CoreData's own reserved, read-only `NSManagedObject.isDeleted` bookkeeping (a same-shaped differently-named field survived a save fine; `isDeleted` never did) — fixed by renaming the *live* `@Model` property to `isPendingDeletion` on all three types (the `.kudosbackup` JSON field name and `KudosBackupWork/Collection/ReadingQueue` struct fields are unaffected — they're plain `Codable` structs, not `@Model`, so `isDeleted` stays as the on-disk key for backward compatibility); (2) the `.kudosbackup` encoder used plain `.iso8601` date encoding, which has no fractional-second support and silently truncates every timestamp to the whole second — two edits landing in the same wall-clock second (a real possibility with fast automated sync, and not impossible with real auto-sync either) became unorderable by every `lastModifiedAt`-based merge decision in the file; fixed with a custom formatter (`.withFractionalSeconds`) on encode, with a fallback to the old whole-second formatter on decode so older backups still read correctly. Also fixed: `PreservedWorkService.softDelete`/`restore` weren't calling `markModified()`, so the soft-delete state couldn't win real sync merge conflicts (found via the two-device convergence test). Added `PreservedWorkTests` (soft-delete field/EPUB-retention, restore/tombstone-retraction, queue soft-delete preserves memberships, sweep date-gating + gate no-op, sweep hard-deleting a queue tombstones memberships and updates `isQueuedForLater`, AO3-unavailable confirmation-copy escalation, manifest v7 round-trip, `incomingWins` gating, full two-device soft-delete→restore convergence). Verified: `Scripts/lint.sh` clean, `git diff --check` clean, full iOS suite green (178 passed, up from 168), iOS Simulator build, macOS Debug build. **Adversarial review + fix pass (Claude, pre-merge):** a line-by-line review of the branch diff found two real gaps, both fixed in the follow-up commit. **(1) Ghost rendering:** the `@Query` exclusions covered every list view, but relationship-derived listings weren't filtered — a soft-deleted work still rendered fully visible/readable inside `ReadingQueueDetailView`/`ReadingQueueCard` (via `queue.memberships`) and `CollectionDetailView`, and inflated `CollectionCard`/`AddToCollectionView` counts (via `collection.works`); the old hard-delete's cascade removed memberships so this was a real regression, not cosmetic. Memberships/links still survive for restore — they're now filtered at render time only. **(2) Re-acquiring a Recently Deleted work never restored it:** every acquisition path matched the hidden pending record — `DownloadQueue` marked a fresh Save "skipped" (user saves, nothing appears, the work stays scheduled for permanent deletion), `WorkDetailView` adopted the hidden record, `ReadingQueueService.addToSavedForLater`/`addAndPreserveSummary`/`preserveSeries` queued ghosts the sweep would later hard-delete out from under the queue, `importUserEPUB` reported "duplicate" while the only copy sat in Recently Deleted, and `importEPUB` (the shared AO3-download funnel) has **no dedup at all**, so paths that did proceed would insert a hidden-twin duplicate that collides on restore. Fixed with one consistent policy — *re-acquiring revives*: `importEPUB` now revives a pending-deletion source match in place (keeping reading progress, swapping in the fresh EPUB) instead of inserting a twin; `DownloadQueue` restores an EPUB-bearing pending match with no download at all (EPUB-less ones fall through to the download and revive via `importEPUB`); `WorkDetailView` deliberately does *not* adopt a pending match (page shows remote state; Save revives); the three queue-service acquisition paths and `WorkCardActions.resolveLocalWork` restore before use; `importUserEPUB` reports `.restored`. **(3) A crash class the new tests exposed:** the fire-and-forget `WorkTags.refreshFromAO3` task crashes with SwiftData's invalidated-model assertion if the work is deleted (or, in tests, the whole container torn down) before/while it runs — its very first line reads `work.ao3WorkID`. Added `modelContext != nil` liveness guards at entry, after the network await, and in the 404 catch (the same guard pattern `PersistenceMigrationService` already uses). **Minor:** `RecentlyDeletedView.daysRemaining` now rounds up (an hour after deleting it said "89 days left"); Settings' saved-works→queue migration now excludes Recently Deleted works. Added 3 regression tests (revive via `addToSavedForLater`; revive-not-duplicate via `importEPUB`; `.restored` via `importUserEPUB`). Re-verified after fixes: `Scripts/lint.sh` clean, `git diff --check` clean, full iOS suite green (181 passed, up from 178), macOS Debug build. **Manual remaining:** interactive tap-through of the Recently Deleted UI in a live simulator (screen-control permission was denied this session too) — logic is covered by `PreservedWorkTests`; the TASKS.md UI-consistency approval gate (screenshots + human approval) still applies before merge. | Claude | `feature/preserved-work-recovery` | (this commit) | 2026-07-05 |
| T-69 | **BGTaskScheduler background refresh for Library Sync Folder** — best-effort background sync freshness on top of the existing reliable launch/foreground/background/debounced sync (never a replacement for it). New `FolderSyncBackgroundTask` (iOS-only) registers a `BGAppRefreshTask` at `MyApp.init()` (before the scene attaches, per Apple's requirement — `MyApp` now builds an explicit shared `ModelContainer` instead of using the `.modelContainer(for:)` scene-modifier convenience), reuses `FolderSyncService.syncNow(in:)` unchanged (already gated by `PersistenceOperationGate`, so a rejection from contention completes quietly, no retry storm), and only submits a request when `FolderSyncSnapshot.isConnected && autoSyncEnabled` (`FolderSyncBackgroundTask.shouldSchedule(snapshot:)`, unit tested). `scheduleNext()` is called after launch's catch-up sync and again on `.background`/`.inactive` scene-phase transitions (re-arming, since a `BGAppRefreshTask` is one-shot per submission). **Info.plist blocker found and fixed:** `GENERATE_INFOPLIST_FILE=YES`'s `INFOPLIST_KEY_*` mechanism silently drops array-valued keys (`UIBackgroundModes`, `BGTaskSchedulerPermittedIdentifiers`) — confirmed via build + `plutil -p`; combining `INFOPLIST_FILE` with `GENERATE_INFOPLIST_FILE=YES` is a hard "Multiple commands produce Info.plist" error. Landed on a `PBXShellScriptBuildPhase` that injects both keys via `PlistBuddy` post-generation, iOS/iOS-Simulator only (`PLATFORM_NAME` guarded, no-op on macOS). Two further build-system issues surfaced and were fixed before this actually worked: (1) the phase had no declared `inputPaths`/`outputPaths`, so Xcode's build-graph scheduler had no ordering edge and could run it racing the internal Info.plist generation step — fixed by declaring `$(TARGET_BUILD_DIR)/$(INFOPLIST_PATH)` as both an input and an output; (2) `ENABLE_USER_SCRIPT_SANDBOXING=YES` (already on for this project) silently sandbox-denies a Run Script phase's writes to any path outside its declared outputs — `PlistBuddy` printed "Operation not permitted" but still exited 0, so the phase's own success echo was meaningless; the script now verifies the keys actually landed (`PlistBuddy -c Print`) and fails the build loudly if not, instead of trusting `PlistBuddy`'s exit code. A same-path input+output also requires a second, harmless dummy output (`BackgroundModesInjected.stamp`) since Xcode errors on a "mutable output but no other virtual output node" otherwise. Verified end-to-end: `plutil -p` on the built iOS Simulator `Info.plist` shows both keys present; macOS Debug build's `Info.plist` correctly has neither (script no-ops there); simulator install + launch clean (log shows `(BackgroundTasks) Handling app launch`, confirming registration succeeded). Added `FolderSyncBackgroundTaskTests` for the pure `shouldSchedule` gating logic (`BGTaskScheduler` itself isn't unit-testable). Verified: `Scripts/lint.sh` clean, `git diff --check` clean, full iOS suite green (168 passed, up from 167), iOS Simulator build + install + launch, macOS Debug build (`Scripts/build-macos.sh`). **Manual remaining:** real background-refresh firing (Xcode's "Simulate Background App Refresh" debug menu, and eventually real-device timing) — the pure gating logic is covered, but the OS's actual scheduling decision isn't something a unit test can exercise. | Claude | `feature/folder-sync-bg-refresh` | (this commit) | 2026-07-05 |
| T-68 | **No-entitlement Library Sync Folder** — replaced staged entitlement/CloudKit assumptions with a user-selected Library Sync Folder that stores `KudosLibrary.kudosbackup`, persists a folder bookmark, syncs down/up with `NSFileCoordinator`, folds file-provider conflicts through backup restore, gates folder sync against migration/import, and exposes Settings controls/disclosure. Backup manifest v6 now includes collections alongside works, queues, bookmarks, fonts, settings, and tombstones. Launch/scene-phase/debounced local changes trigger conservative folder sync when connected. Added deterministic temp-folder tests for package write/read, sync-down restore of works/queues/collections, two-device convergence, missing-file no-op, operation-gate reentrancy, conflict-content folding, and sync-up timestamp stability. Docs/README now describe folder-based iCloud Drive sync honestly; no entitlement/container/CloudKit APIs or `.entitlements` file were added. Verified `rg` forbidden API audit, `find . -name "*.entitlements"`, `git diff --check`, `Scripts/lint.sh`, focused `FolderSyncTests`, full iOS suite (`Scripts/test.sh 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17,OS=26.5'`), and macOS Debug build. **Manual remaining:** real-device iCloud Drive folder/provider behavior, quota/offline/conflict UX, and large-library timing. **Adversarial review + fix/completion pass (Claude):** a 6-lens review against both the transport prompt and the separate full-automatic-sync-and-onboarding prompt found this commit implemented transport well but left several required pieces of the second prompt missing or buggy; all fixed/added here. **Fixed:** (1) foreground `.active` sync-down had no throttle — added a 60s gate (bypassed by explicit dirty state); (2) background/inactive sync-up ran unconditionally on every transition even with zero changes, forcing a needless full-file rewrite/iCloud re-upload — now gated on a real durable `hasPendingFolderSyncChanges` flag (`FolderSyncService.markDirty()`/`isDirty`), cleared only after an actual write, checked again on next launch to catch anything a force-quit dropped mid-debounce; (3) `runGuarded()`'s gate-rejection throw bypassed `recordError()`, so a sync silently lost to contention left zero trace anywhere — fixed so it's always recorded; (4) reader close had zero folder-sync wiring at all — added a near-immediate (1.5s, not the full 7s debounce) best-effort sync-up on `onDisappear`; (5) collection membership merge was additive-only (pure union) with no way to represent an intentional removal, so an older sync file could silently re-add a work the user removed from a collection — added a `.workCollectionMembership` tombstone type (composite `(collectionID, workID)` ID) wired into both removal call sites in `Collections.swift`, consulted during restore with the same newer-wins escape hatch used elsewhere; (6) `WorkCollection.isDeleted` merged via unconditional boolean AND (currently dead code, but the same unguarded-boolean bug class fixed for `SavedWork` previously) — gated on `incomingWins` to match. **Added (previously entirely missing):** Auto Sync Settings toggle (default on once connected; automatic triggers respect it, manual Sync Now always works regardless); a first-install Library Sync Folder onboarding screen (`SyncFolderOnboardingView`, shown once after the existing `WelcomeView`, never in place of it) with Choose Sync Folder / Not Now / Don't Remind Me Again, gated by `hasConfiguredSyncFolder`/`hasPermanentlyDismissedSyncFolderOnboarding` (a session-only in-memory flag handles "remind me next launch" without touching either persisted flag); a lightweight Sync Details sheet in Settings (status + last result, kept out of the main list); collections/collection-conflict counts in `KudosBackupRestoreSummary` and the Settings import notice, matching the existing queue-conflict reporting. **Deferred, documented rather than silently skipped:** `BGTaskScheduler` background-refresh registration was not implemented this pass — foreground/lifecycle/debounced sync covers the primary reliable path, and background refresh would only improve freshness at the margin; worth adding later since it's a standard `UIBackgroundModes` capability and (unlike CloudKit) does not require a paid Apple Developer account. Added 8 regression tests (gate-rejection error visibility, dirty-flag clears only after a real write, collection-membership tombstone suppresses a stale resurrection, a demonstrably-newer snapshot still revives it, collection-level suppression is reported in the restore summary). **Also fixed a real, pre-existing test-suite flake found while verifying:** `PersistenceOperationGate` is a process-wide static lock (correctly global for the real app, since only one instance runs), but `xcodebuild`'s default parallel test execution runs `PersistenceSyncTests`/`KudosBackupTests`/`FolderSyncTests` concurrently in separate simulator clones, letting them spuriously contend the same gate and non-deterministically fail (`PersistenceMigrationService.run()` silently no-ops and returns `.notStarted` when the gate is already held) — reproduced consistently across repeated full-suite runs. Fixed by adding `-parallel-testing-enabled NO` to `Scripts/test.sh` (the project's own canonical verification command) plus `@Suite(.serialized)` on the two suites that weren't already marked. Verified clean across 3 consecutive full-suite runs post-fix. Verified: `Scripts/lint.sh` clean, iOS build clean, full iOS suite green (167 passed / 0 failed, up from 153; deterministic now, was previously flaky under the default parallel runner). | Codex, Claude | `codex-icloud-drive-folder-sync` | (this commit) | 2026-07-04 |
| T-67 | **Queue tombstone conflict fix + iCloud sync staging** — added default-safe queue deletion metadata, queue membership freshness, and backup v4 queue/member timestamps; `.kudosbackup` restore now computes `effectiveQueueModifiedAt`, suppresses only stale queue/member snapshots, revives newer queue metadata or membership activity over older tombstones, preserves/reports ambiguous conflicts, and never falls back suppressed memberships into Saved for Later. Settings import summaries now report suppressed/revived/ambiguous queue conflicts. iCloud remains honestly staged: no entitlement/container or real-device CloudKit verification is claimed; docs list the blockers and manual device checklist. Added deterministic queue conflict/migration tests. Verified `git diff --check`, `Scripts/lint.sh`, focused Persistence/Backup xcodebuild tests, full iOS suite (`Scripts/test.sh 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17,OS=26.5'`), and macOS build. **Adversarial review + fix pass (Claude):** found and fixed two real gaps this commit had — (1) `effectiveQueueModifiedAt`/`effectiveModifiedAt` folded the backup file's own `exportedAt` wall-clock time into the revival decision, so a content-stale queue could be revived purely because the file it shipped in was written later than the tombstone (fixed by dropping `exportedAt`/`restoreOrImportDate` from the calculation entirely — only the queue's/memberships' own timestamps count now). (2) `SyncTombstone` rows never traveled in the `.kudosbackup` manifest at all, so a fresh install/reinstall restoring a backup had zero local tombstone history and no protection against resurrecting anything deleted after that backup was made — the single most common real-world use of `.kudosbackup` (device migration/reinstall). Fixed via manifest v5: tombstones now export with the backup and merge into the target's local store on restore, before conflict resolution runs, so a fresh device inherits the source device's deletion history. Added 2 regression tests for both fixes. Verified: lint clean, iOS build clean, full iOS suite green. | Codex, Claude | `codex-icloud-sync-queue-conflict-fix` | (this commit) | 2026-07-04 |

Backup schema v3 now preserves sync/progress metadata with timestamp-aware merge rules; EPUBs keep stable asset identifiers and remain local/manual-backup assets until a signed iCloud build is tested. Settings shows local persistence readiness and iCloud account availability. Added persistence migration/merge/tombstone tests and docs. Verified `git diff --check`, `Scripts/lint.sh`, iOS build (`iPhone 17`, OS 26.5), full iOS tests (`Scripts/test.sh 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17,OS=26.5'`), and macOS build. **Adversarial review + fix passes (Claude, `e8a6164` + follow-up):** backup import now consults tombstones (was write-only — older backups could resurrect explicitly-deleted works, including privacy-cleared history) with a newest-tombstone-wins index and a newer-snapshot escape for works; members of a suppressed queue are dropped, not re-homed into Saved for Later; isFavorite/isSaved/isFinished/isComplete no longer OR-merge (older backups resurrected explicitly-unset flags) while brand-new records still adopt archive flags; `WorkLifecycle.delete` tombstones cascade-deleted queue memberships; `PersistenceMigrationService` is async with yield points + a re-entrancy guard + deleted-model guards (Settings retry no longer blocks the main thread); stage-aware migration error logging; Settings disclosure states nothing syncs to iCloud in this build. Six regression tests added. **Known toolchain issue (pre-existing, tracked separately):** Release-config builds (macOS + iOS device archive) crash the beta Swift compiler inside vendored SwiftSoup (`Element.appendNormalisedText` SIL ownership verifier); Debug builds unaffected. | Codex, Claude | `backup-refinement-icloud` | (this commit) | 2026-07-04 |

## 🐛 Bugs (BUG-N registry)

_Consolidated from the former `docs/Bugs.md`._ **Status:** Open · In Progress · Fixed. Detail for each is in the Completed table / `git log` under its board task.

**Active:**
- **BUG-9** — (**P1**; found 2026-07-25 while owner-testing T-148's checklist. Filed only — not fixed, per owner decision; no code written.) **Top-level comments silently post to the wrong chapter on multichapter works.** `AO3WriteActions.postComment(workID:content:expectedGeneration:onFormPrepared:)` (`Services/AO3WriteActions.swift:43`) takes **no chapter parameter at all**: it scrapes the form from `commentFormURL(workID:)` = `/works/:w?view_adult=true` and POSTs to `commentsEndpoint(workID:)` = `/works/:w/comments`. Since `/works/:w` renders **chapter 1** for a multichapter work, every top-level comment is attached to chapter 1. `AO3CommentContext.chapterID` exists and is threaded through `CommentsModel` (`chapterForRequests` → `startComposer`'s context), but it is used **only** for draft keying and the `CommentSubmissionGuard` key — `CommentsModel.swift:804` calls `postComment` without it, so it never reaches the request. **User-visible consequence:** reading chapter 20 in By Chapter scope, writing a comment, and having it land publicly on chapter 1 of someone else's work — wrong-target public writes, which is why this is P1 despite being long-standing. Replies are unaffected (`postCommentReply` posts to `/comments/:parentID/comments`, which AO3 resolves to the parent's own chapter), as is the reader's per-chapter Comments sheet for *reading*; only top-level composition mis-targets. **Owner-reported UI symptom that surfaced it:** "the 'Write a Comment' window on 'All Comments' should display a dropdown to select which chapter the comment is being posted to" — correct, and note the dropdown is necessary but **not sufficient**: with the POST ignoring chapter, a picker would appear to work while every comment still went to chapter 1, which is worse than no picker. **Proposed fix (unverified, see caveat):** add `chapterID` to `postComment`; when non-nil, scrape the form from `/works/:w/chapters/:c?view_adult=true` and POST to `/chapters/:c/comments`; pass `context.chapterID` at `CommentsModel.swift:804`; then add the composer chapter picker, defaulting to an explicit chapter so the target is always visible rather than implied (the existing `chapterPicker` sheet + `model.chapters`/`selectedChapter` in `CommentsView.swift` are reusable). ⚠️ **The `/chapters/:c/comments` endpoint shape is inferred, not verified** — from this repo's own consistent commentable routing (`/works/:id/comments`, `/comments/:id/comments` at `AO3CommentActions.swift:330`) plus otwarchive's commentable routes. Per `docs/AO3_NETWORKING_POLICY.md` this needs live-session confirmation before it ships, and confirming it means **posting real comments to a real work** — do it against a work the owner controls, not a third party's. Track: B2 (comments correctness), not a HIG wave.
- **BUG-7** — (P2; found live in T-115's macOS pass, 2026-07-18) The legacy macOS reader lays its WKWebView content out **under the overlaid sidebar**: with the sidebar visible, the leading edge of every preface/metadata line is occluded ("~~Post~~ed originally on…", "~~Teen A~~nd Up Audiences", "~~Categor~~y:") and content centers relative to the full window rather than the detail column. Reproduced on "The Queen's Mercy". `Features/Reader/ReaderView.swift`/`ReaderController.swift` (macOS-only path) — the web view needs to respect the split-view detail column / sidebar safe area. Evidence: T-115 report §3.9.
- **BUG-6** — (pre-existing, low) Newest-first comments: after posting, the refresh sizes "last page" from the cached page count (`CommentsModel.knownTotalPages`), so a comment that rolls AO3 over onto a new page (every 20th top-level comment) isn't shown until a manual refresh. Found during the 2026-07-10 comment-ui-refinement review; not introduced by that branch. Fix idea: skip the cached count when `forceRefresh` is set.

**Closed — not a bug (owner decision):**
- **BUG-8** — (found live in T-115's macOS pass, 2026-07-18; closed 2026-07-19, Wave 0) macOS Library cards opening the reader directly is **working as intended** — the owner confirmed iOS does the same live on-device, and the code agrees: every Library card call site passes `openMode: .reader` (or navigates straight to `LocalWorkDestination.reader`) on both platforms — `LibraryView.swift:692,699`, `LibrarySectionListView.swift:313,327`, `Collections.swift:133`, `ReadingQueues.swift:285`. The "Library cards open detail" note under Key Decisions was stale (predated this behavior or was never accurate for the carousel/dashboard cards) and has been corrected there. No code change.

**Fixed & verified:**
- **BUG-5** — Opening Browse ballooned CPU + memory until iOS killed the app (owner-reported on iPhone 17 Pro). **Two root causes, fixed separately.** (1) View-side: `MediaBrowserView.stats(for:)` built a `Set` of each big media category's full (tens-of-thousands) fandom-name list and scanned the whole library, synchronously in the view body, per card, re-run on every render as `@Observable` fandom lists streamed in (~O(categories²×fandoms) on main). Moved off render+main: cards read a precomputed `statsByCategory`; a debounced `.task(id:)` snapshots works to `Sendable` structs and computes each name-`Set` once in `Task.detached`. (`685c8a3`.) (2) **Parser-side (the on-device killer, still present after fix 1):** `AO3Client.fandoms(atPath:)` SwiftSoup-parsed each multi-MB `/media/<name>/fandoms` page into a full DOM, then ran `li.select("a.tag")` **per `<li>`** (14k+ for the big categories) — each select triggering the vendored SwiftSoup's `rebuildQueryIndexes…`/`traverseElementsDepthFirst` over a several-hundred-thousand-node tree, ×3 concurrent categories. **Reproduced empirically in the sim** (cold fandom cache): RSS 330MB→**1.33GB** in ~10s, CPU pegged ~100% — the sim survives the transient churn, a phone jetsams mid-load. And because `FandomCatalog` persisted only after the whole task group, a mid-load kill lost every fetched list → the next open repeated the full burst (kill **loop**). Fixed: new `AO3Client.parseFandomIndex(_:)` — single linear cursor scan (no DOM), CSS-faithful class matching (`classList(in:)`), entity-decoded names, digits-only counts, deduped — **byte-identical results to the DOM parse on real AO3 pages** (Movies 14,496 / Anime & Manga 5,079 in back-to-back runs of both parsers); `FandomCatalog.loadMissing/refresh` now persist per landed category. Measured after: same cold-cache open peaks at **427MB RSS** (~340MB baseline) with sub-second CPU blips; Browse cards fill normally. +3 parser fixture tests. (`9ab14c2` on `merge-test`, `Scripts/verify.sh` green 282/33 iOS+macOS, 2026-07-10. **Owner: rebuild/reinstall on device to pick this up.**)
- **BUG-4** — Library bulk-select `EditMode` broke the macOS build; guarded `#if os(iOS)`, macOS uses a plain list (T-43, 2026-06-21).
- **BUG-3** — AO3 login was discarded when Keychain was unavailable; now falls back to WebKit's app-scoped cookie store (T-31, 2026-06-20).
- **BUG-2** — T-09 tag-cycling UI + tag-picker search-field placement regression (T-22, 2026-06-18).
- **BUG-1** — Sepia theme not applying app-wide; fixed via `.appThemedScroll()`/`.appThemedRows()` (T-08, 2026-06-18).

## ✨ UI Polish (UI-N registry)

_Consolidated from the former `docs/UI_Polish_Todo.md`._
- **UI-1** — Refined the Search pagination pill: elevated card, tightly grouped page pills, long-press arrows jump to first/last, nearby-page fallback on narrow cards (Done; T-14/T-25).

_UI-2 … UI-9 are T-115's confirmed HIG findings (all adversarially verified against source; **0 P1 / 24 P2 / 28 P3**). Per-finding file/line evidence, HIG citations, and verifier notes: [`docs/audits/HIG_ADHERENCE_REVIEW_T115.md`](docs/audits/HIG_ADHERENCE_REVIEW_T115.md) §3. Status: all Open._

- **UI-2** — (P2) **VoiceOver labeling & grouping sweep.** The canonical patterns exist in-repo (OnboardingPointRow, SensitiveWorkRow, TextSizeSlider); these sites missed them: `WorkStatLabel`/`CardMetaLabel` raw HStacks (every card's stats read as fragments); comment-thread reply structure invisible (spine chrome hidden, no substitute label); Inbox rows = 5 overlapping buttons; `RecentlyDeletedRow` ungrouped; CustomizeTheme sliders unlabeled + swatch selection color-only (no `.isSelected`); `selectableRow`/`cyclingFacetRow` missing traits; MatureContent blurred branches use bare `onTapGesture` + swallow the nested expand button; `CardNavigationModifier`'s opacity-0 NavigationLink likely adds a stray stop to every card (needs VO runtime check); TagChip/BugReport/AuthorProfile/WorkDetail-tile smaller items. Umbrella: 126/160 files have zero a11y modifiers (16 control-heavy). Report §3.1.
- **UI-3** — (P2) **Sub-44pt hit targets.** Inbox bulk-bar icons (32pt), SearchPaginationBar buttons (31pt, arrows fixed-frame), `WorkRowExpandButton`, WorkDetail Remove-tag icon (tag chips in the same file got the 44pt fix), tappable author bylines app-wide (glyph-sized), `ReorderHandle` (28pt = absolute floor, sole reorder path, no hint), carousel-section collapse + see-all chevron, Comments pagination/sort. Report §3.2.
- **UI-4** — (P2) **Dynamic Type fixed frames.** Fixed 164×228pt cover cards truncate nearly every field at AX sizes (**runtime-confirmed**; `WorkStatLabel` also chains `lineLimit(1)`+`fixedSize()`); Work Detail's segmented control has no AX fallback (the quick-action grid in the same file shows the pattern); hero fandoms `lineLimit(3)` no expand; reader progress pill fixed 40pt; see-all grid hard-codes 2 columns (never widens on iPad/macOS); Account shortcut grid scales text down instead of reflowing. Report §3.3 + §5.1.
- **UI-5** — (P2) **Reduce Motion coverage.** Only 3 files gate on `accessibilityReduceMotion` (all correctly) vs 15 files animating. Ungated spatial sites: comment expand/highlight + animated scrollTo + Browse toast `.move`, carousel collapse `withAnimation(.snappy)`, iOS reader-chrome fade (file already reads the env var for a sibling), CustomizeTheme, stats `numericText`. Suggested: small `withReducedMotion` helper + sweep. Report §3.4 + §5.2.
- **UI-6** — (P2) **Theming/color-role hygiene.** Sepia segmented-control theming = global UIKit appearance proxy with hardcoded RGB (can't adapt to Increase Contrast/Reduce Transparency); excluded-tag state hardcodes `.red` ×3 (Sepia contrast unverified, no semantic role); `BugReportView` is the sole grouped-Form outlier missing `.appThemedRows()`/`.appThemedScroll()` (two-line fix); destructive-role misuse (Reset Filters, collection "Remove", unconfirmed context-menu-only Clear All Filters). Report §3.5.
- **UI-7** — (P2) **Consistency.** Bulk-select implemented twice, incompatibly — macOS dashboard has no select mode while the section list one level down has a cross-platform one (port the Bool-based pattern up); selection mode hides the tab bar non-modally to mask a Liquid Glass bleed (fix the bleed, not the chrome); hub chrome tokens diverge (Account 20pt everywhere vs Work Detail 16pt + stray 14pt, despite a sibling-parity comment; Author Profile picker = third shell); `chevron.right` on rows that don't push (segmented-tab jump, sheet-opening rows); pagination bar rendered twice with identical a11y labels; sheet-inside-inspector on iPhone. Report §3.6.
- **UI-8** — (P2/P3) **Timing magic numbers as completion signals.** 350ms `Task.sleep` sequences modal teardown → next push at 3 sites (CommentsView ×2, AuthorProfileView); 700ms sleep suppresses same-touch author-nav double-push in AppRouter. Replace with onDismiss continuations / deterministic signals. Report §3.7.
- **UI-9** — (P3) **Platform-split code health.** The two globally-`#if os(macOS)` reader files contain nested `#if os(iOS)` blocks that can never compile — and the dead copy already diverged (`.presentationContentInteraction(.scrolls)` exists only there, not in the live iOS sheet); shared `letterSpacingRange`'s negative half renders on macOS but is clamped on iOS. Report §3.8.

## 🧭 Key decisions & open questions

- **macOS reader — decided:** iOS/iPadOS use Readium; **macOS keeps the legacy
  reader** (Readium navigator is UIKit-only). Readium SPM is scoped `platformFilter = ios;`.
- **Workflow — decided:** **single `main` branch** (the `main`/`readium-migration`
  split was consolidated 2026-06-24). Just commit to `main`; no cross-branch porting.
- **AO3 authentication — decided:** native account UI drives AO3's real form in
  a hidden WebView; mechanism failures reveal the same WebView as a fallback.
  Sessions, never passwords, are stored device-only in Keychain.
- **Comment connectors moving under a swipe — decided (2026-07-29, owner call):**
  **live with it. Do not hand-roll the swipe.** Observed on device (iOS 26.5):
  `.swipeActions` translates the *whole* row, `listRowBackground` included — there
  is no per-row layer that stays put, so any connector that has to line up with a
  neighbouring row visibly desyncs mid-swipe (the swiped row's segment slides, the
  unswiped one doesn't). This also falsifies the older claim, now corrected in
  `CommentRowChrome.body`, that a swipe "translates content only".
  The only fix that keeps cross-row lines is replacing `.swipeActions` with a
  custom gesture so the lines can sit outside the translated layer (which is what
  the `AO3 Comments.dc.html` concept does). **Rejected:** it means re-implementing
  full-swipe, snap points, haptics and the VoiceOver rotor integration that
  `.swipeActions` provides for free, and the failure modes there are worse than
  the cosmetic one being fixed. Note this is a cost of **cross-row connectors
  specifically** — the Flat style has none, so choosing Flat closes the issue
  outright rather than tolerating it. Do not re-litigate without new information.
- **Team ID in `project.pbxproj` — decided (2026-07-19, Wave 0):** keep
  `DEVELOPMENT_TEAM = NQH85H7343` as-is (2 of 4 build configs; the other 2 are
  already `""`). Owner call: the exposure is low-value (identifies the Apple
  Developer team, doesn't grant signing) and not worth a git-history rewrite. No
  scrub, no history purge. Closes A1-F2 / T-48 / KD-TEAMID — no action needed.
- **Library metadata — decided (2026-07-19, Wave 0):** split by platform. iOS
  consolidates onto **Readium's** metadata handling (Readium is iOS-only,
  `platformFilter = ios`). **macOS keeps the custom OPF layer** (legacy reader —
  no Readium navigator there to source metadata from). Closes KD-MIGRATION-META.
- **`KudosBackupManifest.supportedVersions` — decided (2026-07-19, Wave 0):** raise
  the floor to the latest version only — no pre-July `.kudosbackup` exports are
  still in use. `Services/KudosBackup.swift:174-175` changes from
  `[1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, currentVersion]` to `[currentVersion]` (currently `7`).
  Closes PONY-6.7. **Not yet implemented** — one-line change, unscheduled.
- **Compact/Detailed default — decided (2026-07-19, Wave 0):** keep `@AppStorage`
  semantics as-is; no forced migration for users who already launched under the
  old `.detailed` default (SHORT-R5). **New scope item:** the compact/detailed
  toggle should become **one app-wide setting**, not per-screen. Today it's **5
  independent stores** that don't share state: `AccountView.swift:33`
  (`@AppStorage("account.displayMode")`, default `.compact`),
  `HomeSectionListView.swift:36` (`@AppStorage("home.\(kind).displayMode")`,
  **keyed per Home section**, default `.detailed`), `LibrarySectionListView.swift:44`
  (same pattern, **keyed per Library section**), `ReadingQueues.swift:107` (plain
  `@State`, **not persisted at all** — resets every visit), plus
  `AccountComponents.swift:606` / `AuthorProfileContentSections.swift:194,364`
  (externally-driven parameters, no storage of their own). Fix: one shared
  `@AppStorage` key (or an observable settings object) read by all of them,
  default `.compact`. Unscheduled — natural fit for Wave 1 (shared foundation),
  same "one helper, many call sites" shape as X1–X6.
- **Cleanup — done:** `test/card-lists` (abandoned/polluted) was deleted in the
  2026-06-24 single-branch consolidation, along with all other non-`main` branches.
- **Layout overhaul (`test/home-tab-overhaul`) — decided:** Home & Library both use
  the shared `WorkCarouselSection`. **Library cards open the reader directly**,
  same as Home — confirmed live on iOS and macOS 2026-07-19 (Wave 0); this
  supersedes the earlier "Library cards open detail" note, which was stale (see
  BUG-8 in the Bugs registry). **Saved for Later = local saved + AO3 Marked for
  Later** merged; **Collections = placeholder** (no model yet); **no Synced/Local
  badges yet**. (Branch long since merged to `main` and deleted in the
  single-branch consolidation.)
- **Open — layout follow-ups (deferred):** (1) light filter *quick-chips* on the
  Library dashboard — only the active-filter banner + full Filters inspector exist
  so far; (2) a `Collection` model to make Collections real.

## UI Consistency & Density Audit (Required Before Merge)

### Purpose
Ensure new UI work remains consistent with the established Kudos design language and does not regress information density, scanability, or theme behavior.

### Human Verification Required
The following items require screenshots and human review before completion:

- AO3 account sub-picker
- Library bulk-select mode
- Download queue banner
- About page
- Continue Reading shelf
- Search result cards
- Library cards

### Information Density Review
For every new or modified card:

- Verify title visibility is unchanged or improved
- Verify author visibility is unchanged or improved
- Verify fandom visibility is unchanged or improved
- Verify reading progress visibility is unchanged or improved
- Verify chapter/word count visibility is unchanged or improved
- Verify download status visibility is unchanged or improved
- Verify no critical metadata has been hidden behind additional taps

### Theme Consistency Review
Verify all new UI components:

- Use ThemeManager/AppThemeSurface
- Support Light, Dark, and Sepia themes
- Avoid hardcoded colors
- Avoid hardcoded corner radius values
- Avoid hardcoded shadows
- Follow existing spacing scale

### Card Family Consistency
Compare against existing Library/Search cards:

- Typography hierarchy
- Metadata presentation
- Badge styling
- Progress indicators
- Padding and spacing
- Corner radius treatment

### Approval Gate
Before merging UI changes:

1. Screenshot review completed
2. Density review completed
3. Theme review completed
4. Human approval received

Do not mark UI tasks complete until all four requirements are satisfied.
